2.22
) on Green’s functions to obtain

\displaystyle|u^{\varepsilon}_{bl,i}(x)|\leq

\displaystyle\,C\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{x\cdot n}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{d}}\,|\nabla u^{0}(\tilde{x})|d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{x\cdot n}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{d}}\,\,d\tilde{x}

(3.33)

with
C
depending on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
A
.
Further, since
2\cdot((d/2)+\delta)=d+2\delta>d
, the Sobolev embedding theorem gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,\delta)\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{W^{2,d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})},

\displaystyle\leq C(d,\delta)\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}.

(3.34)

Hence, taking into account the fact that there exists
C>0
depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{x\cdot n}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{d}}\,d\tilde{x}\leq C<\infty,

(3.35)

one obtains

\displaystyle|u^{\varepsilon}_{bl,i}(x)|\leq

\displaystyle\,C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\delta)\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})},\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in\mathbb{H}_{n}.

(3.36)

Step 3 : estimating
u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}
in the
L^{\infty}
-norm

From relation (
3.6
) defining
r^{\varepsilon}
we get

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x)=\varepsilon\chi(x/\varepsilon)\nabla u^{0}(x)+\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon}_{bl}(x)+r^{\varepsilon}(x),\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in\mathbb{H}_{n},

which implies

\displaystyle|u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x)|\leq\varepsilon|\chi(x/\varepsilon)|\,|\nabla u^{0}(x)|+\varepsilon|u^{\varepsilon}_{bl}(x)|+|r^{\varepsilon}(x)|,\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in\mathbb{H}_{n}.

The estimates in
L^{\infty}
-norm obtained in the preceeding lines allows then to write

\displaystyle|u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x)|\leq C\varepsilon\,\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}+C\varepsilon\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}}+C\varepsilon\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}}.

(3.37)

Finally, we take (
3.34
) into account in inequality (
3.37
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}\leq C\,\varepsilon\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

(3.38)

where
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\delta)>0
with
\delta\in(0,d/2)
.
Here ends the proof of Proposition
3.1
.∎

4.
Homogenization of Green’s function
G^{\varepsilon}

The purpose of this section is to establish a quantitative homogenization result on the Green function
G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})
. In other words, we show that the heterogenous kernel
G^{\varepsilon}
converges to the homogenized one
G^{0}(
as
\varepsilon
vanishes. This fact is precisely stated in the following Proposition whose proof is based on a duality argument.

Proposition 4.1
.

For arbitrary
x\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
and for all
s\in\left(\frac{1}{d+\frac{1}{d}},\frac{1}{d-1+\frac{2}{d}}\right)
there exists
C>0
such that for all
\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq C\varepsilon^{s},\quad\mbox{ for all }\tilde{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}\quad\mbox{with }\quad 1/3\leq|x-\tilde{x}|\leq 5/3.

The constant
C
in this estimate depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}
and
\varepsilon>0
and hold them fixed. We set

D_{x}:=\{\tilde{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}:1/3<|x-\tilde{x}|<5/3\}.\qquad\mbox{ (see {Figure} \ref{domain_D_x}) }

(4.1)

The function
\tilde{x}\mapsto|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|
is continuous on
\overline{D}_{x}
which is compact. Therefore there exists
\tilde{x}_{0}\in\overline{D}_{x}
satisfying
|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})|=S(x,\varepsilon)

with

\displaystyle S(x,\varepsilon):=\sup\ \{|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|:\tilde{x}\in\overline{D}_{x}\}.

From the estimates

\displaystyle|\nabla G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq\;\frac{C}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{d-1}},\quad|\nabla G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq\;\frac{C}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{d-1}},\quad\mbox{ for all }x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{H}_{n},

with
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}),

we derive the existence of a constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})>0
such that

\displaystyle|\nabla G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})|+|\nabla G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq C_{1},\quad\mbox{ for all }\tilde{x}\in A(x,1/6,2):=\{z\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}:1/6<|x-\tilde{x}|<2\}.

Now let
\rho:=\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}C_{1}}
and suppose
\rho\in(0,1/6).
In fact one can always increase
C_{1}
so that
\rho<1/6
. With such a
\rho
one has

\displaystyle B(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)\subset B(x,2)\char 92\relax\overline{B(x,1/6)}.

Figure 2.
Domain
D_{x}
.

Step 1- lower bound :
There exist
i,j\in\{1,...,d\}
such that for all
\tilde{x}\in D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)
we have

\displaystyle|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\geq\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}.

(4.2)

The norm of matrices that we consider here is the maximum norm. From the equality

\displaystyle|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x_{0}})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})|=S(x,\varepsilon)

we obtain the existence of
i,j\in\{1,...,d\}
satisfying

\displaystyle|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})|\geq\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{d^{2}}.

(4.3)

Now let such integers
i,j
be fixed. For all
\tilde{x}\in D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)
we have either
|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\geq\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}
or
|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|<\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}
. We show that the latter condition leads to a contradiction. Indeed, it implies

\displaystyle 0<\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{d^{2}}-|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|

and by use of
inequality (
4.3
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{d^{2}}-|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|

\displaystyle\leq|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})|-|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|

\displaystyle\leq|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})-G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})|+|G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{0})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\|\nabla G^{\varepsilon}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(A(x,1/6,2))}+\|\nabla G^{0}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(A(x,1/6,2))}\right\}|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\rho=\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}.

So the condition
|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|<\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}
yields

\displaystyle 0<\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{d^{2}}-|G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}

which implies (
4.2
).

Step 2 :
Let
\varphi\in C^{1}_{c}(B(0,1))
with
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
and
\varphi(\tilde{x})=1
for
|\tilde{x}|<1/2
and define
\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}
by
\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}(\tilde{x})=\varphi(\frac{\tilde{x}-\tilde{x}_{0}}{\rho})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\in C^{1}_{c}(B(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho))\quad\mbox{ and }\quad 0\leq\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\leq 1,\quad\|\nabla\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}\leq\frac{C}{\rho}.

(4.4)

Let
i,j
be the integers given by Lemma
4
. The inequality (
4.2
) together with intermediate value theorem imply that
G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})
has a constant sign on
D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)
. This sign can be assumed to be positive,
G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})\geq 0
. Then (
4.2
) implies

\displaystyle G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})\geq\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}},\mbox{ for all }\tilde{x}\in D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho).

(4.5)

Now consider the solutions
u^{\varepsilon}
and
u^{0}
of the respective problems (
3.1
) and (
3.4
) with source term
f
defined by
f(\tilde{x})=\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}(\tilde{x})e_{j}
. Using Green representations of both solutions one obtains

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x))_{i}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{(G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x}))f(\tilde{x})\}_{i}d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)}(G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x}))\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}(\tilde{x})d\tilde{x}

and since
\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}(\tilde{x})=1
, for
|\tilde{x}-\tilde{x}_{0}|<\rho/2
, one deduces

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x))_{i}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho/2)}G_{ij}^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G_{ij}^{0}(x,\tilde{x})\;d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho/2)}\frac{S(x,\varepsilon)}{2d^{2}}d\tilde{x}.

Then by the definition of
\rho
we get

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x))_{i}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho/2)}C_{1}\rho\;d\tilde{x}=C_{2}\rho(\rho/2)^{d}=C_{2}\rho^{d+1}.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{0}(x))_{i}\geq C_{2}\rho^{d+1}

(4.6)

with
C_{2}=C_{2}(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})>0
.
Rewriting estimate (
3.8
) with this specific
f
yields

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\|f\|_{W^{1,d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\left\{\|\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\|_{L^{d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}+\|\nabla\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\|_{L^{d/2+\delta}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\left\{\left(\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)}\|\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\|^{d/2+\delta}_{\infty}d\tilde{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}+\left(\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)}\|\nabla\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}\|^{d/2+\delta}_{\infty}d\tilde{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}\right\}

for
\delta\in(0,d/2)
and
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\delta)
.
Then one employs the bounds of
\varphi_{\tilde{x}_{0},\rho}
and of its derivative given in (
4.4
)

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\left\{\left(\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)}\!\!1\,d\tilde{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}+\left(\int_{D(\tilde{x}_{0},\rho)}\frac{C}{\rho}\,d\tilde{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\left\{\left(C\rho^{d}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}+\left(C\rho^{d-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{d/2+\delta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon\rho^{\frac{2d-2}{d+2\delta}}.

The above lines are summarized in

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\rho^{\frac{2d-2}{2\delta+d}}

(4.7)

with
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\delta)>0
with
\delta\in(0,d/2)
.

Connecting inequality (
4.6
) to (
4.7
) we get

\displaystyle C_{2}\rho^{d+1}\leq\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\rho^{\frac{2d-2}{2\delta+d}}

(4.8)

from which we extract

\displaystyle C_{2}\rho^{d+1}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\rho^{\frac{2d-2}{2\delta+d}}

which in turn yields

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{s},\>\mbox{ with }s:=\frac{1}{d+\frac{2+2\delta-d}{2\delta+d}}

(4.9)

with the constant
C
depending on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
\delta
. Recalling the definition of
\rho
one sees that

\displaystyle S(x,\varepsilon)=2d^{2}C_{1}\rho\leq C\varepsilon^{s}

with
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\delta)
.

By the definition of
S(x,\varepsilon)
this last inequality implies

\displaystyle|G^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})-G^{0}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq C\varepsilon^{s},\quad\mbox{ for all }\tilde{x}\in D_{x}\mbox{ and for all }s\in\left(\frac{1}{d+\frac{1}{d}},\frac{1}{d-1+\frac{2}{d}}\right)

where
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})
.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

For
d\geq 3
the range of
s
in (
4.9
) includes
1/d
and the case
s=1/d
corresponds to
\delta=d/2-1
. Moreover, Proposition
4.1
continues to hold for the adjoint Green functions i.e. we also have

\displaystyle|G^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)-G^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|\leq C\varepsilon^{s},\quad\mbox{ for all }(x,\tilde{x})\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}\times\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}\mbox{ with }1/3<|x-\tilde{x}|<5/3

where
C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})
.

5.
Asymptotic expansion of the Poisson kernel
P=P(y,\tilde{y})

In this section we aim at establishing an asymptotic expansion of the Poisson kernel
P=P(y,\tilde{y}
) as
|y-\tilde{y}|\rightarrow\infty
. Seeing that this kernel is expressed in terms of Green’s functions, its expansion can be obtained via those of Green’s functions. In a first step we establish expansions of
(\nabla G^{*,\varepsilon},\Pi^{*,\varepsilon})
in powers of
\varepsilon
. Then, in a next step we apply the uniform estimate (
2.11
) to control these expansions.

By restricting the domain in (
2.15
) the adjoint Green’s functions

(G^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x),\Pi^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x))
satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\,\nabla_{\tilde{x}}G^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)+\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\Pi^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)&=&0,\quad\tilde{x}\in D_{x},\\
\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\cdot G^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)&=&0,\quad\tilde{x}\in D_{x},\\
G^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)&=&0,\quad\tilde{x}\in\Gamma_{x},\\
\end{array}\right.

(5.1)

where
\Gamma_{x}:=D_{x}\cap\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
and
D_{x}
is defined in (
4.1
).

5.1.
Expansion of Green’s functions

We apply the expansions (
2.4
), (
2.5
) to Green’s functions
(G^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x),\Pi^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x))
satisfying (
5.1
) and by doing so we include boundary layer correctors
(u_{bl}^{*},p_{bl}^{*}):

\displaystyle G_{ki}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)=G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\varepsilon(u_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}),

(5.2)

\displaystyle\Pi_{i}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)=\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\pi^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\pi^{*,\alpha}_{k}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+S_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})

(5.3)

where functions
\chi^{*},\pi^{*},\Gamma^{*}
and
Q^{*}
are the interior correctors for the adjoint operator
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{*}
given in Remark
2.4
and for fixed
\beta,j\in\{1,...,d\}
the function
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}=((u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj})_{k}
together with
(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}
are boundary layer correctors solving

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(\tilde{y})\>\nabla(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}+\nabla(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}&=&0&\mbox{ in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n},\\
\nabla\cdot(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}&=&0&\mbox{ in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n},\\
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}&=&-\chi_{j}^{*,\beta}&\mbox{ on }\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

(5.4)

Remark 5.1
.

By the regularity of
A
and
\chi
we have

\displaystyle\|u^{*}_{bl}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n})}\leq C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})<\infty.

(5.5)

Notice that the remainder terms
W^{\varepsilon}
,
S^{\varepsilon}
in the respective expansions are

\displaystyle W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=G_{ki}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)-

\displaystyle G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

(5.6)

and

\displaystyle S_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=\Pi_{i}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)-

\displaystyle\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\pi^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon\pi^{*,\alpha}_{k}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

(5.7)

5.1.1.
Estimates on the error terms
(\nabla W^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})

Here
we find out the equations satisfied by the pair
(W^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})
.
Taking into account the fact that

[\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{*}(G_{i}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x))+\nabla\Pi_{i}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)]_{r}=0

one obtains
the following equation satisfied by
the pair
(W^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})

[\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{*}(W_{i}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla S_{i}^{\varepsilon}]_{r}=F^{\varepsilon}+F_{bl}^{\varepsilon},\quad\tilde{x}\in D_{x},

where

\displaystyle F^{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-1}F^{(-1)}+F^{(0)}+\varepsilon F^{(1)}+\varepsilon^{2}F^{(2)}\quad\mbox{and }\quad F^{\varepsilon}_{bl}:=\varepsilon^{-1}F^{(-1)}_{bl}+F^{(0)}_{bl}+\varepsilon F^{(1)}_{bl}+\varepsilon^{2}F^{(2)}_{bl}.

Let us analyze each order separately. First we have

\displaystyle F^{(-1)}:=

\displaystyle[\{\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}](\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+[\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)](\tilde{x},\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\{\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}A^{*,\gamma\beta}_{rj}(\tilde{y})\}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)](\tilde{x},\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)

and

\displaystyle F^{(-1)}_{bl}:=

\displaystyle[\{\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)](\tilde{x},\tilde{x}/\varepsilon).

These terms of order
\varepsilon^{-1}
are both zero,
F^{(-1)}=F^{(-1)}_{bl}=0
, according to equations (
2.2
) and (
5.4
).
Some of the terms of order 0 in
\varepsilon
involve boundary layer quantities
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj},(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}
whereas others do not. We shall gather the terms following this difference. The quantity
F^{(0)}
gathers the terms of order
0
in
\varepsilon
without a boundary layer term:

\displaystyle F^{(0)}:=

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x))-\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

Using the fact that
\nabla\cdot A^{*,0}\,\nabla G_{i}^{*,0}=\nabla\Pi_{i}^{*,0}
in
D_{x}
and some change of indices we get

\displaystyle F^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}(A^{*,\alpha\beta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x))-\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}(A^{*,0})^{\alpha\beta}_{rk}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+A^{*,\alpha\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x))-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

Then, factorizing the second-order derivative of the Green function yields

\displaystyle F^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\left(A^{*,\alpha\beta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})+A^{\alpha\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))-(A^{*,0})^{\alpha\beta}_{rk}\right)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

Now one can see that
F^{(0)}=0
by the first equation of (
2.3
).

The terms of order 0 in
\varepsilon
involving the boundary layer correctors

u^{*}_{bl},p^{*}_{bl}
are gathered in

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl}:=F^{(0)}_{bl,0}+F^{(0)}_{bl,1}+F^{(0)}_{bl,2}+F^{(0),*}_{bl}.

The term
F^{(0)}_{bl,0}
corresponds to the quantities involving zeroth-order derivatives of
u^{*}_{bl}
and it is

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl,0}=[\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(

\displaystyle A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y}))](u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}\pi^{*,\alpha}_{l}(\tilde{y})(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

By some change of indices and factorization of
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)
we find

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl,0}

\displaystyle=\{\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y}))-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}\pi^{*,\alpha}_{l}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rl}(\tilde{y})\}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle=0

by the first equation of (
2.2
).
Then the quantity
F^{(0)}_{bl}
reduces to

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl}:=F^{(0)}_{bl,1}+F^{(0)}_{bl,2}+F^{(0),*}_{bl}

(5.8)

where the term
F^{(0)}_{bl,1}
relates to first-order derivatives of
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}
and it reads

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl,1}:=

\displaystyle[A^{*,\alpha\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})+A^{*,\delta\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle+[\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y}))+A^{*,\delta\gamma}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-\pi^{*,\alpha}_{l}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{r}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x),

the quantity
F^{(0)}_{bl,2}
involves a second-order derivative of
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}

\displaystyle F^{(0)}_{bl,2}:=A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

and
F^{(0),*}_{bl}
is the term containing the pressure component of the boundary layer

\displaystyle F^{(0),*}_{bl}:=

\displaystyle-(p_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

The terms of order 1 in
\varepsilon
without boundary layer terms form

\displaystyle F^{(1)}:=

\displaystyle\;[A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(A^{*,\delta\gamma}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))+A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

(5.9)

The quantities of order 1 in
\varepsilon
involving boundary layer terms are gathered in

\displaystyle F^{(1)}_{bl}:=\;

\displaystyle F^{(1)}_{bl,0}+F^{(1)}_{bl,1}

(5.10)

with
F^{(1)}_{bl,0}
involving the quantity
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}
without any of its derivatives

\displaystyle F^{(1)}_{bl,0}:=\;

\displaystyle[A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})+A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kl}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(A^{*,\delta\gamma}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kl}(\tilde{y}))](u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-\pi^{*,\beta}_{k}(\tilde{y})(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

and
F^{(1)}_{bl,0}
containing first-order derivatives of
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}

\displaystyle F^{(1)}_{bl,1}:=\;

\displaystyle[A^{*,\delta\gamma}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kl}(\tilde{y})+A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kl}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

Finally, the terms of order 2 in
\varepsilon
are

\displaystyle F^{(2)}:=

\displaystyle A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mbox{and }\quad F^{(2)}_{bl}:=

\displaystyle A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

(5.12)

As for the equation of the divergence, let us first remind that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\cdot G^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x)=\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\cdot G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)=\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}=\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot\chi^{*,\beta}_{j}=\partial_{y_{k}}\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}=\partial_{y_{k}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}=0.

Then a straightforward computation consisting mainly of expanding the right-hand-side of

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\left(G_{ki}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)-G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right)

gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left((u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\left(\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right).

The first line on the right hand side is zero according to the second equation of (
2.3
). Then we obtain

\displaystyle H^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left((u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\left(\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right).

(5.13)

Using the cancellations
G_{ki}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)=\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)=0=G_{ki}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)
,  for
\tilde{x}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
,
the boundary value is
\varphi^{\varepsilon}=(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{k})_{k}
defined by

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)-\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

(5.14)

To summarize, the pair
(W^{\varepsilon}\!,S^{\varepsilon})
satisfies the system of equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\,\nabla W^{\varepsilon}+\nabla S^{\varepsilon}&=&F^{(0)}_{bl}+\varepsilon\left(F^{(1)}+F^{(1)}_{bl}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\left(F^{(2)}+F^{(2)}_{bl}\right),&\tilde{x}\in D_{x},\\
\nabla\cdot W^{\varepsilon}&=&H^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}),&\tilde{x}\in D_{x},\\
\vskip 7.22743ptW^{\varepsilon}&=&\varphi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}),&\tilde{x}\in\Gamma_{x},\end{array}\right.

(5.15)

with the functions
F^{(0)}_{bl},F^{(1)},F^{(1)}_{bl},F^{(2)},F^{(2)}_{bl},h^{\varepsilon}
and
g^{\varepsilon}
defined respectively as in (
5.8
), (
5.9
), (
5.10
), (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.13
) and (
5.14
).

Now we apply the uniform Lipschitz estimate of Theorem
2.6
to the system (
5.15
). To that end, we introduce the domain

D^{0}_{x}:=\{\tilde{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}:2/3<|x-\tilde{x}|<4/3\}\subset D_{x}.

Theorem
2.6
yields the following inequality

\displaystyle\|\nabla W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x}^{0})}+\osc_{D_{x}^{0}}\;S^{\varepsilon}\leq C\left\{\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}\right.

\displaystyle+\|F^{(0)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|H^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+[H^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\,(\|F^{(1)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(1)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})})

(5.16)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}(\|F^{(2)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(2)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})})

\displaystyle+\|\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+\|\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\,\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+\left.[\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\;\varphi^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}\right\}

for
l>0
and where
C
depends on
d,l,\eta,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}
.
We aim at showing that the right hand side of this inequality is of size
\varepsilon^{\kappa}
for some
\kappa>0
. Therefore, in what follows we are going to establish a bound of the form
C\varepsilon^{\kappa}
to each of the terms in the right hand side of (
5.16
). Let us begin with the term
\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

We have the following estimate for
W^{\varepsilon}
:

\displaystyle\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/d},

where the constant
C
depends essentially on
A
and on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

Proof.

The term
\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}
is bounded as follows. By the definition of
W^{\varepsilon}
and by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle|W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})|\leq

\displaystyle\;|G^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)-G^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|+\varepsilon|\chi^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)+u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|\cdot|\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}|\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|+\varepsilon^{2}|\chi^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|,\quad\mbox{ for all }\tilde{x}\in D_{x}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}\leq\>

\displaystyle\|G^{*,\varepsilon}(\cdot,x)-G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+\varepsilon\,(\|\chi^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|u^{*}_{bl}\|_{\infty})\|\nabla G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left(\|\Gamma^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|\chi^{*}\|_{\infty}\|u_{bl}^{*}\|_{\infty}\right)\|\nabla G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.1
together with the Remark that follows it, and by the boundednes of the correctors given in Lemma
2.3
along with the facts that
\nabla G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)
is bounded in
D_{x}
and
\|u_{bl}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})<\infty
, we get

\displaystyle\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1/d}

with
C
depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Here we would like to clarify some tools used in the preceding proof and that we might use again.

(1)

We implicitely use the bound
\|\partial^{\lambda}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}\leq\sup_{\tilde{x}\in D_{x}}\frac{C}{|\tilde{x}-x|^{d-2+|\lambda|}}=C(1/3)^{d-2+|\lambda|}
for a multi-index
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}^{d},
with
|\lambda|=1,2,3,4,
throughout our study. The constant
C
in this estimate depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.

(2)

In the previous proof and on other occasions we use abbreviations of the type

\chi^{*}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0},\quad u_{bl}^{*}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0},\quad\Gamma^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0},\quad\chi^{*}u_{bl}^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}.

Let us give the meanings of these notations

\displaystyle\chi^{*}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}

\displaystyle=\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0},\qquad u_{bl}^{*}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}=(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0},\qquad Q^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}=Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}

\displaystyle\chi^{*}u_{bl}^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}

\displaystyle=\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0},\quad\pi^{*}u_{bl}^{*}:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}=\pi^{*,\alpha}_{k}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}:\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}.

Lemma 5.4
.

We have the following estimate for
H^{\varepsilon}
where
H^{\varepsilon}
is defined in
(
5.13
)

\displaystyle\|H^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+[H^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta}

where
C
depends on
d
,
\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle H^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left((u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\left(\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right).

By Lemma
2.3
and (
5.5
) this identity implies

\displaystyle\|H^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}\leq\>

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+\varepsilon\|\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\|\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\chi(\cdot/\varepsilon)u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\>

\displaystyle C\varepsilon.

Now we estimate the Hölder semi-norm of
H^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle[H^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>\varepsilon[u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}+\varepsilon[\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}[\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}+\varepsilon^{2}[\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}.

These semi-norms in the right hand side are estimated as follows : e.g. for
[u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle[u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>[u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\|\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle+\|u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}[\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>(1/\varepsilon)^{\eta}[u_{bl}^{*}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{x})}\|\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle+\|u_{bl}^{*}\|_{L^{\infty}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{x})}[\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

where
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{x}:=\{\tilde{y}=\tilde{x}/\varepsilon:\tilde{x}\in D_{x}\}
. We then obtain

\displaystyle[u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\varepsilon^{-\eta}

with
C
depending on
A
and
d
. Similarly, one has

\displaystyle[\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\varepsilon^{-\eta}

\displaystyle[\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\varepsilon^{-\eta}

\displaystyle[\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{3}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\varepsilon^{-\eta}

where
C
depends on
A
and on
d
.
Thus we get

\displaystyle[H^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\varepsilon\varepsilon^{-\eta}+\varepsilon^{2}\varepsilon^{-\eta})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\varepsilon^{1-\eta}.\qed

As for the boundary data in system (
5.15
) we have the following Lemma

Lemma 5.5
.

We have

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+\|\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\,\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+[\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\;\varphi^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta}

where the constant
C
depends on
A,d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.3
and Remark
5.3
and estimate (
5.5
) we have a straightforward bound on
g^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}=

\displaystyle\left\|-\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)-\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.
Each term in the derivative of
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
is either of order 1 or of order 2 in
\varepsilon
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\,\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\varphi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}\ \leq C\varepsilon

with
C
depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.
Finally, the Hölder semi-norm related to the boundary value is bounded as follows

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\;\varphi^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\nabla\left(-\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)-\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)u^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}\left[\nabla\left(\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\nabla\left(\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)u^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}.

The terms in the right hand side of this above inequality are bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left[\nabla\left(\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle=[\varepsilon^{-1}\nabla_{y}\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)+\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}[\nabla_{y}\Gamma^{*}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma_{x})}\|\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{-1}\|\nabla_{y}\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}[\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{-\eta}[\Gamma^{*}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma_{x})}\|\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}\left[\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}.

So we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left[\nabla\left(\Gamma^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left[\nabla\left(\chi^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)u^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\!:\!\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right)\right]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta},

with
C
depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.

Consequently, we finally get

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\;\varphi^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}.
∎

Further, we have the following estimates for source terms in the first equation of (
5.15
).

Lemma 5.6
.

Functions
F^{(1)},F^{(1)}_{bl},F^{(2)},F^{(2)}_{bl}
satisfy

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\|F^{(1)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(1)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\right)\leq C\varepsilon

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left(\|F^{(2)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(2)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.3
the functions
A^{*}
,
\chi^{*}
,
\pi^{*}
,
\Gamma^{*}
and
Q^{*}
and their derivatives are bounded by a constant
C
depending on
\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
. The quantity
\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)
is bounded by a constant depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
according to Remark
5.3
. Moreover the domain
D_{x}
is bounded. Therefore each term in

\displaystyle F^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\;[A^{*,\gamma\alpha}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})+\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(A^{*,\delta\gamma}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y}))+A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle-Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)

(5.17)

is in
L^{d+l}(D_{x})
. For instance, we have

\displaystyle\int_{D_{x}}\left|A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\delta}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right|^{d+l}d\tilde{x}\leq C^{d+l}|D_{x}|<\infty

where the constant
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
. Similarly each term of
F^{(2)}
or
F^{(2)}_{bl}
is in
L^{d+l}(D_{x})
.
∎

Now the remaining terms are those in
F^{0}_{bl}.
Using estimates on the derivative
\nabla u_{bl}^{*}
of the boundary layer corrector one can show that the
L^{d+l}
norm of these terms are
O(\varepsilon^{\kappa})
, for some
\kappa>0
. In fact, we have the following.

Lemma 5.7
.

For
l>0,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|F^{(0)}_{bl}\right\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/(2(d+l))}.

where
F^{(0)}_{bl}
is defined in
(
5.8
)
and where
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
defined in
(
5.18
)
.

We apply the following fact in the proof of the above Lemma.

Lemma 5.8
.

For
k=1,2
, there exists a constant
C(d,\mu,\eta,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},k)>0
,
\eta\in(0,1)
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\|\nabla^{k}u_{bl}^{*}(M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))\|_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,+\infty)}\leq C<+\infty.

The proof of Lemma
5.8
is given in the Appendix as proofs of Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
.

The bound in this Lemma can be rewritten more explicitly as follows: for
k=1,2,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla^{k}u_{bl}^{*}(M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))\|^{2}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla^{k}u_{bl}^{*}(M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))|^{2}dz_{d}\leq C<+\infty.

In particular, one has

\displaystyle M_{2}:=\left(\sup_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{T_{h}}|\nabla u^{*}_{bl}(Mz)|^{2}dz\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}\!\!\!<\infty,\quad T_{h}:=(0,1)^{d-1}+h,\ h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}.

(5.18)

Lemma 5.9
.

We have the following estimates on the pair
\left(u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon),p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\right)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}\leq M_{2}\,\varepsilon^{1/2}

(5.19)

and

\displaystyle\|p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}

(5.20)

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
M_{2}
.

Proof.

This is an application of Lemma
A.6
.

Step 1-Control of the velocity
u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)

Recall that
D_{1}(0):=\{\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{H}_{n}:|\tilde{y}|<1\}
and
D_{1/\varepsilon}(0):=(1/\varepsilon)D_{1}(0)
. On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}(0)}|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|^{2}d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{d}\int_{D_{1/\varepsilon}(0)}|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y}|^{2}d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{d/2}\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}\|_{L^{2}(D_{1/\varepsilon}(0))},

(5.21)

on the other hand an application of estimate (
A.13
) with
r=1/\varepsilon
and
q=2
yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla u^{*}_{bl}\|_{L^{2}(D_{1/\varepsilon}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\,\varepsilon^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-d/2}.

Then by the equality (
5.21
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}\leq M_{2}\,\varepsilon^{1/2}.

Step 2-Control of the pressure
p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)

First, remark that

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}(0)}|p_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|^{2}d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{d}\int_{D_{1/\varepsilon}(0)}|p_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y}|^{2}d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\|p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{d/2}\|p_{bl}^{*}\|_{L^{2}(D_{1/\varepsilon}(0))}.

(5.22)

Then one applies estimate (
A.14
) with
r=1/\varepsilon
and
q=2
to get

\displaystyle\|p^{*}_{bl}\|_{L^{2}(D_{1/\varepsilon}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varepsilon^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-d/2}.

Hence, the equality (
5.22
) yields

\displaystyle\|p^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{1}(0))}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2},

(5.23)

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
M_{2}
. ∎

In the above proof of Lemma
5.9
the size and the center of the domain
D_{1}(0)
have the sole role of simplifying the notation. Therefore, by that Lemma we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{2}(x))}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\quad\mbox{and }\>\|p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{2}(x))}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2},\quad\mbox{for all }x\in\mathbb{H}_{n}.

Moreover, seeing that
D_{x}\subset D_{2}(x)
(see (
4.1
)) we also have

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\quad\mbox{and }\>\|p_{bl}^{*}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\|_{L^{2}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}.

(5.24)

Proof of
Lemma 10
.

Recall that
F^{(0)}_{bl}=F^{(0)}_{bl,1}+F^{(0)}_{bl,2}+F^{(0)}_{bl,*}
. The first term
F^{(0)}_{bl,1}
contains quantities involving first order derivatives of
u_{bl}^{*}
such as
A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)
. They are estimated as follows. By Lemma
2.3
and by Remark
5.3
there is a constant
C
depending on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{D_{x}}|A^{*,\gamma\delta}_{rk}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\delta}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\gamma}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)|d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{D_{x}}|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|d\tilde{x}.

Now by using Hölder inequality together with (
5.24
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{D_{x}}|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\leq|D_{x}|^{1/2}\left(\int_{D_{x}}|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)|^{2}d\tilde{x}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq|D_{x}|^{1/2}M_{2}\,\varepsilon^{1/2}.

We then have obtained

\displaystyle\|(A^{*}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*})(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{1}(D_{x})}\leq C(d,M_{2})\ \varepsilon^{1/2}.

Now one makes an interpolation of this last estimate with

\displaystyle\|(A^{*}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*})(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}=O(1)

to get

\displaystyle\|(A^{*}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}u_{bl}^{*})(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/(2(d+l))}.

The term
F^{0}_{bl,2}
is estimated similarly by using Lemma
5.8
with
k=2
.

The term
F^{(0),*}_{bl}=-(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)
is bounded as follows. By Remark
5.3
there exists a constant
C
depending on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\partial_{\tilde{x}_{r}}\partial_{\tilde{x}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x)\right\|_{L^{1}(D_{x})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{D_{x}}\left|p^{*}_{bl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\right|d\tilde{x}.

By Hölder’s inequality and by estimate (
5.20
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{D_{x}}\left|p^{*}_{bl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\right|d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\leq|D_{x}|^{1/2}\left(\int_{D_{x}}\left|p^{*}_{bl}(\tilde{x}/\varepsilon)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
M_{2}
.
Then one ends up with the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|p^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{1}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
. Making an interpolation between the above
L^{1}
estimate and estimate

\displaystyle\left\|p^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}=O(1)

one obtains ​​​

\displaystyle\left\|p^{*}_{bl}(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/(2(d+l))}

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
.
∎

Summary.

At the beginning we had the inequality (
5.16
)
then we have successively established the following estimates on the terms in the right hand side of (
5.16
)

\displaystyle\|W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1/d},

\displaystyle\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x})}+[h^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta},

\displaystyle\|g^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+\|\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\,g^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{x})}+[\nabla_{\!\!\textup{ tan}}\;g^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\eta},

\displaystyle\|F^{(0)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1/(2(d+l))},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\|F^{(1)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(1)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left(\|F^{(2)}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}+\|F^{(2)}_{bl}\|_{L^{d+l}(D_{x})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

where
C
depends on
A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
.
The number
\eta\in(0,1)
can be taken small enough so that
1-\eta\geq\,1/(2(d+l))
. We then arrive at the conclusion

\displaystyle\|\nabla W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x}^{0})}+\underset{D_{x}^{0}}{osc}\;[S^{\varepsilon}]\leq C\varepsilon^{\kappa},\mbox{ with }\kappa=1/(2(d+l)),\quad l>0,

(5.25)

with
C
depending on
A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
.

5.2.
Expansion of the Poisson kernel

We are looking for an asymptotic expansion of the Poisson kernel
P(y,\tilde{y})
when
|y-\tilde{y}|\rightarrow\infty
with
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
,
\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
. Recall that the Poisson kernel is defined as

\displaystyle P=(P_{ij})_{i,j}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad P_{ij}(y,\tilde{y})

\displaystyle=-A_{jk}^{*,\alpha\beta}(\tilde{y})\,\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ki}^{*}(\tilde{y},y)n_{\alpha}(\tilde{y})+\Pi_{i}^{*}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}(\tilde{y})

\displaystyle=-A_{kj}^{\beta\alpha}(\tilde{y})\,\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ki}^{*}(\tilde{y},y)n_{\alpha}(\tilde{y})+\Pi_{i}^{*}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}(\tilde{y}).

We may occasionally write it in the compact form

\displaystyle P(y,\tilde{y})

\displaystyle=-A^{*}(y)\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n(\tilde{y})+\Pi^{*}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n(\tilde{y}).

The expression of the Poisson kernel suggests to seek its expansion through the expansions of
\nabla_{\!\!y}G^{*}
and
\Pi^{*}
. We are going to express the expansions
\nabla W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)\cdot n
and
S^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})
given in (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) in terms of the variables
y,\tilde{y}
. Here the parameter
\varepsilon
determines the scale separation of the two variables. More precisely we have :
y=x/\varepsilon,\;\tilde{y}=\tilde{x}/\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon=\frac{|x-\tilde{x}|}{|y-\tilde{y}|}\simeq\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|}
where the distance
|y-\tilde{y}|
is supposed to be large.

5.2.1.
Rescaling

We have the following estimate expressed in terms of the slow variables
x,\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\|A(\cdot/\varepsilon)\nabla W^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{x}^{0})}+\underset{D_{x}^{0}}{osc}\,[S^{\varepsilon}]\leq C\varepsilon^{\kappa},\mbox{ with }\kappa\in(0,1/2d)\mbox{ and }C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2}).

Now we are looking for a large-scale version of the above estimate, i.e. we wish to express it in the variables
\tilde{y},y\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}
, satisfying
\tilde{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}
where
R:=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|.

Remark 5.10
.

We have

D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}=\{\tilde{y}\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}:\tilde{y}=\tilde{x}/\varepsilon,\tilde{x}\in D_{x}^{0}\}
.

In what follows we compute the rescaled functions
W(\tilde{y})=W^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\tilde{y}),S(\tilde{y})=S^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\tilde{y})
. We proceed by rewriting the expansion (
5.6
) and taking into account the scaling properties given in Lemma
2.10

\displaystyle W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-2}}G^{*}_{ki}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-2}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-\varepsilon\left[\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\left[\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-2}}\left\{G_{ki}^{*}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-G_{ki}^{*,0}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\right.-\left[\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\qquad-\left.\left[\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\!(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\!\!\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\}.

We thus get the following formulation of
W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})
:

\displaystyle W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x}):=W_{ki}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x},x)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-2}}W_{ki}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

with the function
W
defined by

\displaystyle W_{ki}(\tilde{y},y)=

\displaystyle G_{ki}^{*}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-G_{ki}^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-\left[\chi^{*,\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\qquad-\left[\Gamma^{*,\alpha\beta}_{kj}\!\!\left(\tilde{y}\right)+\chi^{*,\alpha}_{kl}\!\!\left(\tilde{y}\right)(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{lj}\!\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right).

This identity yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{\tilde{x}}W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}W\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

and then

\displaystyle A\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{x}}W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}A\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{y}}W\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right).

Consequently, the inequality

\displaystyle\left|A\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{x}}W^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{\kappa}

with
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
,
implies

\displaystyle\left|A\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{y}}W\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{d-1+\kappa}.

Hence one obtains

\displaystyle\left|A\left(\tilde{y}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{y}}W\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1+\kappa}}

(5.26)

where
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
.

We wish to have similar estimates for the pressure term.
Taking into account the scaling properties of Green’s functions and using (
5.7
), we get

\displaystyle S_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}S_{i}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

(5.27)

with

\displaystyle S_{i}(\tilde{y},y):=\Pi_{i}^{*,\varepsilon}(\tilde{y},y)

\displaystyle-\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)-[\pi^{*,\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})+(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)

\displaystyle-[Q^{*,\alpha\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})+\pi^{*,\alpha}_{k}(\tilde{y})(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{x},x).

We rescale the estimate
\underset{D_{x}^{0}}{\textup{osc}}[S^{\varepsilon}]\leq C\varepsilon^{\kappa}
with
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
:

If
\tilde{x},\hat{x}\in D_{x}^{0}
and satisfy

\displaystyle|S^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})-S^{\varepsilon}(\hat{x})|\leq C\varepsilon^{\kappa},

then we have, from (
5.27
),

\displaystyle\left|S\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-S\left(\frac{\hat{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{d-1+\kappa},

hence

\displaystyle\left|S\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-S\left(\hat{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1+\kappa}}.

Thus the oscillation estimate
\underset{D_{x}^{0}}{osc}\ [S^{\varepsilon}]\leq\;C\varepsilon^{\kappa}
with
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
rescales to

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{y},\hat{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}\left|S\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-S\left(\hat{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tilde{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1+\kappa}}

yet the bound in the right hand side corresponds to the inverse of
\inf_{\tilde{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1+\kappa}
which is
R^{d-1+\kappa}
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1+\kappa}}=\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}.

We then obtain the following oscillation estimate of the pressure term in the fast variable

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{y},\hat{y}\in D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}\left|S\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-S\left(\hat{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\osc_{D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}[S\left(\cdot,y\right)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}

(5.28)

where
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
.

Nonetheless this estimate is a local estimate as it is set in the domain
D_{2R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}
whereas we seek for an estimate valid for all
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
,
\tilde{y}\in\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}
with
|y-\tilde{y}|
large. So we extend (
5.28
) to the entire domain
\overline{\mathbb{H}}_{n}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, reasoning on
\tilde{y}_{m},\hat{y}_{m}\in D_{2^{m}R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{2^{m-1}R}(y)}
similarly as above leads to

\displaystyle\osc_{D_{2^{m}R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{2^{m-1}R}(y)}}\>[S\left(\cdot,y\right)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(2^{m-1}R)^{d-1+\kappa}}

where
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
.

Remark 5.11
.

The above estimate remains valid when
D_{2^{m}R}(y)\char 92\relax\overline{D_{2^{m-1}R}(y)}
is replaced with

A_{m}:=\overline{D_{2^{m}R}(y)}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{2^{m-1}R}(y)}.

Now decompose the punctured half-space
\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}
into annuli as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}=\bigcup^{\infty}_{m=1}A_{m}.

Then we get

\displaystyle\osc_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}}\>[S(\cdot,y)]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\>\osc_{A_{m}}\>[S\left(\cdot,y\right)],

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\frac{C}{(2^{m-1}R)^{d-1+\kappa}},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}\sum^{\infty}_{m=0}\frac{1}{(2^{d-1+\kappa})^{m}},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}\quad\mbox{ with }C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2}).

This can be rephrased as : for any
\tilde{y},\hat{y}\in\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax\overline{D_{R}(y)}
we have

\displaystyle\left|S\!\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-S\!\left(\hat{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}\quad\mbox{ with }C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2}).

This estimate combined with the fact that

\displaystyle\lim_{|\hat{y}|\rightarrow\infty}S\!\left(\hat{y},y\right)=0

yields

\displaystyle\left|S\!\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}}.

(5.29)

Now we put together (
5.26
) and (
5.29
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|A(\tilde{y})\nabla W(\tilde{y},y)\right|+\left|S(\tilde{y},y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d-1+\kappa}},

\displaystyle\left|A(\tilde{y})\nabla W(\tilde{y},y)\right|+\left|S(\tilde{y},y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d-1+\kappa}},\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n},\,\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n},

where
C=C(A,d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},M_{2})
.

Large-scale expansion of the Poisson kernel
P=P(\tilde{y},y).

We aim at establishing an asymptotic expansion of
P=P(y,\tilde{y})
when
|y-\tilde{y}|\longrightarrow+\infty
.

Now we search for terms
P_{0},

P_{1}
and
P_{2}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\left|P(y,\tilde{y})-P_{0}(y,\tilde{y})-P_{1}(y,\tilde{y})-P_{2}(y,\tilde{y})\right|\leq\frac{C}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d-1+\kappa}},\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n},\,\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}.

We obtain the expansion for
P
from the formula

\displaystyle P(y,\tilde{y})

\displaystyle=-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n\,+\Pi^{*}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n.

Notice that

\displaystyle-A(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}W(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n+S(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n

\displaystyle=P(y,\tilde{y})-P_{0}(y,\tilde{y})-P_{1}(y,\tilde{y})-P_{2}(y,\tilde{y})

that

\displaystyle A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}W(\tilde{y},y)=

\displaystyle A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\Gamma^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left[\Gamma^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right),

and that

\displaystyle S(\tilde{y},y)=

\displaystyle\;\Pi^{*}(\tilde{y},y)-\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)-[\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})+p_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-[Q^{*}(\tilde{y})+\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y).

The terms that contain a derivative of
G^{*,0}
at the same order are grouped together. The terms of the first-order derivatives of
G^{*,0}
are in

\displaystyle P_{0}(\tilde{y},y)=

\displaystyle\;-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n+\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n

\displaystyle\>-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle\>+\{[\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})+p_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n.

The term corresponding to the second-order derivatives of
G^{*,0}
are in

\displaystyle P_{1}(\tilde{y},y)=

\displaystyle\>-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)

\displaystyle\>-\{A^{*}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\cdot n+\{Q^{*}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n

\displaystyle\>-\{A^{*}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}(\chi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}_{0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\cdot n+\{\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n.

and lastly we set

\displaystyle P_{2}(\tilde{y},y)

\displaystyle=-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n.

By summarizing the preceding lines we arrive at the following statement.

Proposition 5.12
.

For all
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
and for all
\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
we have the following expansion of the Poisson kernel

\displaystyle P(y,\tilde{y})=

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\cdot n+\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n

\displaystyle-\{A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot n

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\{[\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})+p_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left\{\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot n

(5.30)

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left\{\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}[\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{y})+\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot n

\displaystyle+\{[Q^{*}(\tilde{y})+\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n

\displaystyle-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n

\displaystyle+R(y,\tilde{y})

with the remainder
R(y,\tilde{y})
being bounded as

\displaystyle|R(y,\tilde{y})|\leq\frac{C}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d-1+\kappa}},\quad\mbox{ for all }\kappa\in(0,1/2d)

(5.31)

and where the constant
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{2}
defined in
(
5.18
)
.

6.
Convergence towards a boundary layer tail

In this section we aim at establishing the convergence of the solution
u^{s}
to problem (
1.1
) as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty
. First, we establish the convergence in the case
s=0
. Then we show that the parameter
s\in\mathbb{R}
is not relevant in this convergence when
n
is irrational.

6.1.
Convergence of the boudary layer far away from the boundary in the case
s=0

The method that is going to be employed is based on the representation of the solution
u
with the Poisson kernel

\displaystyle u(y)=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}P(y,\tilde{y})g(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}.

(6.1)

The asymptotic expansion (
5.30
) of this kernel will be key for studying the convergence properties.

We take advantage of the fact that the boundary data
g
as well as functions
A^{*},\chi^{*},\pi^{*},\Gamma^{*},Q^{*}
are quasiperiodic on the hyperplane
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
. We do so through an application of the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

(
[
30
]
, Theorem S.3.)

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\longmapsto\mathbb{R}
be a quasiperiodic function then there exists
\mathcal{M}(f)\in\mathbb{R}
such that for all
\varphi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\varphi(y)f\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)dy\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{M}(f)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\varphi(y)dy.

(6.2)

This is a qualitative ergodic theorem for quasiperiodic functions. The hyperplane
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}\sim\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
will play the role of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
in Lemma
6.1
. Notice that the convergence in (
6.2
) can be arbitrarily slow. By using this Lemma we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 6.2

(Theorem
1.1
in the case
s=0
)
.

As
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty
the solution
u=u(y)
to problem
(
1.1
)
with
s=0
converges to a constant vector field
U^{\infty}(n)
with components

\displaystyle U_{i}^{\infty}(n)=

\displaystyle\>\>\mathcal{M}\left(\!-n_{\alpha}g_{j}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\!\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.

(6.3)

Proof.

Injecting in (
6.1
) the expansion of
P(y,\tilde{y})
given by Proposition
5.12
yields

\displaystyle u(y)=

\displaystyle\>U^{0,1}(y)+U^{0,2}(y)+U^{0,3}(y)+U^{0,4}(y)+U^{1,1}(y)+U^{1,2}(y)+U^{1,3}(y)

\displaystyle+U^{2,1}(y)+U^{2,2}(y)+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})g(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

(6.4)

where

\displaystyle U^{0,1}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\cdot ng(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y},\qquad U^{0,2}:=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes ng(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{0,3}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\{\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u^{*}_{bl}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{0,4}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})+p^{*}_{bl}(\tilde{y})\}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes ng(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

are integrals that involve first-order derivatives of
G^{*,0}
and
\Pi^{*,0}
. The other terms involve higher order derivatives of
G^{*,0}

\displaystyle U^{1,1}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\left\{\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{1,2}(y):=

\displaystyle\ \int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{[Q(\tilde{y})+\pi^{*}(\tilde{y})u_{bl}^{*}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{1,3}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left\{-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\Gamma^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{2,1}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left\{-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\Gamma^{*}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U^{2,2}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}.

The superscript
i
in
U^{i,j}
represents the order of the derivative of
\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)
or
\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)
.
Remark first that
the bound (
5.31
) on the remainder and the boundedness of
g
imply

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})g(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{C}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d-1+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(y\cdot n)^{\kappa}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Now we study the limit of each of these terms
U^{i,j}(y)
as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty.

Step 1 : terms
U^{0,1},\,U^{0,2},\,U^{0,3},\,U^{0,4}
.

Step 1-a : Convergence of the term
U^{0,1}

First, we rewrite this quantity into coordinates by considering its
i^{th}
component
