\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{[-A^{*}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\cdot n]\,g(\tilde{y})\}_{i}\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{j}(\tilde{y})\,d\tilde{y}.

For all
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
and by using an estimate in (
2.19
) on Green’s functions we get

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)|\,d\tilde{y}\leq C\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{y\cdot n}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d}}\,d\tilde{y}\leq C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})<\infty.

For arbitrary
R>0
, consider
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
such that its tangential component is bounded by
R

i.e.
y^{\prime}:=y-(y\cdot n)n\in B(0,R)\cap\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
.
Applying the rotation
M
, such that
y=Mz,

z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
, for all
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\{0\}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(M(\tilde{z}))\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}),M(z)\right)g_{j}(M(\tilde{z}))\,d\tilde{z}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0))\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))g_{j}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0))\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

Now we set
\varepsilon:=\frac{1}{y\cdot n}

and use the change of variables
\tilde{z}^{\prime}\rightarrow\tilde{z}^{\prime}/\varepsilon
(this change of variables in the integration does not concern the variable
z=(z^{\prime},z_{d})
)

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M\left(\frac{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\varepsilon},0\right),M\left(z^{\prime},z_{d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(M\left(\frac{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\varepsilon},0\right)\right)g_{j}\left(M\left(\frac{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\varepsilon},0\right)\right)\,\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

Note that
z_{d}=y\cdot n=1/\varepsilon
. Then the linearity of
M
implies

\displaystyle M(z^{\prime},z_{d})=M\left(\frac{\varepsilon z^{\prime}}{\varepsilon},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1).

We use this remark to perform a slight transformation in the integral

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon},\frac{M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)}{\varepsilon}\right)n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

Recall the following rescaling property

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\frac{\tilde{x}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)=\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{x},x\right)

that we apply in the above expression to get

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

We use the following trick to introduce the quantity
G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)
that is independent of
\varepsilon
and plays the role of
\varphi
in Lemma
6.1

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{i}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\!\!

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)\right)

(6.5)

\displaystyle\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\!\!-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\!\left(\!\!\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\!\right)\!d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

We shall first show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\!\!

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}=0

uniformly in
z^{\prime}\in B^{d-1}(0,R).

The mean value inequality gives

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),\right.

\displaystyle M(0,1))-\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon R)}|\nabla_{y}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(w^{\prime},1)\right)||\varepsilon z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon R\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon R)}|\nabla_{y}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(w^{\prime},1))|.

Using a version of estimate (
2.20
)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(\tilde{y},y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}},\quad\mbox{ with }C=C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{y}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),\,M(w^{\prime},1)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}

leading to

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon R\!\!\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon R)}\frac{C}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}.

Taking
\varepsilon
small enough, so that
\varepsilon R\leq 1,
we get

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon R\!\!\!\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{C}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}.

In the present context,
B(0,1)
denotes the unit ball of
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
.
So we have the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon R\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.
Moreover, one has

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}=\int_{B(0,1)}

\displaystyle\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}\char 92\relax B(0,1)}\sup_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

If
\tilde{z}^{\prime}\in B(0,1)
then

\inf_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})=1

and if
\tilde{z}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}\char 92\relax B(0,1)
then

\inf_{w^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}(1+|w^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})=1+(|\tilde{z}^{\prime}|-1)^{2}.

Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}\sup_{u^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|u^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}1\ d\tilde{z}^{\prime}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}\char 92\relax B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+(|\tilde{z}^{\prime}|-1)^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C(d)+C(d)\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{r^{d-2}}{(1+(r-1)^{2})^{d/2}}dr<+\infty.

Finally we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}\leq C\varepsilon R

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and not on
\varepsilon
. We let
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and hold
R
fixed. Then the above estimate directly gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\times n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0\quad\mbox{ as }\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

(6.6)

Now one handles the term

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

through an application of Lemma
6.1
. Recall that the function
-n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}g(M(\cdot,0))g_{j}(M(\cdot,0))
is quasiperiodic in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
. In addition, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\cdot,0),M(0,1)\right)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}).

Now that all the elements required by Lemma
6.1
are in hand one is ready to state that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}

\displaystyle\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}g_{j}(M(\cdot,0)\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}g_{j}(M(\cdot,0))\right)\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)\,d\tilde{y}.

(6.7)

Putting (
6.5
), (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) together we obtain

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,1}(y)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\cdot)g_{j}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\!\!-\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)\,d\tilde{y}.

(6.8)

Step 1-b :  Convergence of the term
U^{0,2}

One focuses on the
i^{th}
component of
U^{0,2}

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,2}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}g_{j}(\tilde{y})\,d\tilde{y}

and apply the method carried out in the study of the convergence of
U^{0,1}
.

Consider
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
such that
y^{\prime}:=y-(y\cdot n)n\in B(0,R)\cap\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
for arbitrary
R>0
.
The above-mentioned method leads to

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,2}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\left(\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)\right)n_{j}g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)n_{j}g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

where
M
is the the rotation defined above and
\varepsilon:=\frac{1}{y\cdot n}
.

First, we are going to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)\right)n_{j}g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\,d\tilde{z}^{\prime}=0

uniformly in
z^{\prime}\in B^{d-1}(0,R).

Using the mean value inequality

\displaystyle|\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(

\displaystyle M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon R)}|\nabla_{y}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(u^{\prime},1)\right)\!|\ |\varepsilon z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon R\sup_{u^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon R)}|\nabla_{y}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(u^{\prime},1))|.

along with the estimate (
2.26
)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(\tilde{y},y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(u^{\prime},1))|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(1+|u^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}

one gets

\displaystyle|\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon R\sup_{u^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{C}{(1+|u^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}

with
\varepsilon
small enough so as
\varepsilon R\leq 1
. Which leads to

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}\left|\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)\right.-

\displaystyle\left.\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))\right|\left|n_{j}g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon R\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}\sup_{u^{\prime}\in B(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+|u^{\prime}-\tilde{z}^{\prime}|^{2})^{d/2}}d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon R.

Hence one obtains the convergence

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d-1}}|\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(\varepsilon z^{\prime},1)\right)-

\displaystyle\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1))|\left|n_{j}g_{j}\left(\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|d\tilde{z}^{\prime}\rightarrow 0

(6.9)

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

Now one applies Lemma
6.1
with the fact that
g(M(\cdot,0))
is quasiperiodic in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
and

\displaystyle\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\cdot,0),M(0,1)\right)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}).

We then get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)

\displaystyle,M(0,1))n_{j}g_{j}\left(\!\frac{M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)}{\varepsilon}\right)d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g_{j}(M(\cdot,0)\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(M(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),M(0,1)\right)d\tilde{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g_{j}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{z}^{\prime}.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,2}(y)\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g_{j}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)\,d\tilde{z}.^{\prime}

(6.10)

Step 1-c : Convergence of the term
U^{0,3}

We study the convergence of the term

\displaystyle U^{0,3}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-A(\tilde{y})\{\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

which we rewrite into coordinates as

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,3}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}[\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)]\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}.

Seeing that the quantity

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

is of the form

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-B_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)d\tilde{y}

where
B_{j}^{\beta}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\longmapsto\mathbb{R}
,
B_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y}):=n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}(\tilde{y})g_{l}(\tilde{y})
is periodic, one can apply the same reasoning that has led to (
6.8
). Then one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-B_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)d\tilde{y}\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}-\mathcal{M}(B_{j}^{\beta}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)d\tilde{y}.

As for the term

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

we point out that the function
(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}
may not be periodic and one can not directly apply the argument that has just been carried out on the term involving
\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}
. Nonetheless, it is quasiperiodic on the hyperplane
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
(by Appendix
D
) then it can be written

\displaystyle(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})=V_{kj}^{*,\beta}(N\tilde{z}^{\prime},0),\quad\mbox{ for }\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n},

(6.11)

where
V_{kj}^{*,\beta}(\theta,t),\,\theta\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\,t>0,
is periodic in
\theta
.
From this identity, we find that the derivative
\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}
is quasiperiodic on
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
. Then we obtain the convergence similarly to the preceding cases

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)g_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)d\tilde{y}.

In conclusion, we have the following convergence for the term
U_{i}^{0,3}

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,3}(y)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})\right]\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)d\tilde{y}.

(6.12)

Step 1-d : Convergence of
U^{0,4}

We treat this quantity

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,4}(y):=

the same way we have dealt with
U^{0,3}
.
Since the function
\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}
is quasiperiodic on the hyperplane
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
, Lemma
6.1
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)\,n_{r}g_{r}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.

As for the term

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)\,n_{r}g_{r}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

one uses the quasiperiodicity of the pressure
p^{*}_{bl}
along

, given in Appendix

,

(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})=(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(N(\tilde{z}^{\prime},0))=R_{j}^{*,\beta}(N\tilde{z}^{\prime},0)

is periodic in

. Then by Lemma

one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)\,n_{r}g_{r}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.

In conclusion, we have the convergence of
U^{0,4}

\displaystyle U_{i}^{0,4}(y)\xrightarrow{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\left[\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.

(6.13)

Step 2 : Terms
U_{i}^{1,1}(y)
,
U_{i}^{1,2}(y)
,
U_{i}^{1,3}(y)

U_{i}^{2,1}(y)
,
U_{i}^{2,2}(y)
.

We show that these terms converge to zero when
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty.
We use the estimate (
2.27
) on the derivatives of Green’s functions
G^{*,0}
associated with the constant coefficients operator

\displaystyle|U^{1,1}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left|A(\tilde{y})\left\{\left[\chi^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+u_{bl}^{*}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng(\tilde{y})\right|d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left|\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right|d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}d\tilde{y}.

From estimate (
3.35
) one gets

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}d\tilde{y}\leq\frac{C}{y\cdot n}

from which we draw

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}|U^{1,1}(y)|\leq\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{C}{y\cdot n}=0.

(6.14)

So are the limits of the remaining terms:

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U^{1,2}(y)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U^{1,3}(y)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U^{2,1}(y)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U^{2,2}(y)=0.

(6.15)

Now we arrive at a conclusion : from (
6.4
) (
6.8
), (
6.10
), (
6.12
), (
6.13
), (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u_{i}(y)=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U_{i}^{0,1}(y)+\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U_{i}^{0,2}(y)+\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U_{i}^{0,3}(y)+\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}U_{i}^{0,4}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\>\mathcal{M}\left(\!-n_{\alpha}g_{j}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\!\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g_{l}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g_{r}(p^{*}_{bl})^{\beta}_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.\qquad\qquad\qed

6.2.
Convergence in the case of general
s\in\mathbb{R}

The previous theorem gives the limit, as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty
, of the half-space boundary layer
u^{s}
defined on the half-space
{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s)
in the particular case
s=0
. Next, we are going to investigate what the limit would be in the case of an arbitrary
s\in\mathbb{R}.
Recall that for
s\in\mathbb{R}
the pair
(u^{s},p^{s})
denotes the solution to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{y}\cdot A(y)\>\nabla_{y}u^{s}+\nabla_{y}p^{s}&=&0&\mbox{in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s),\\
\nabla_{y}\cdot u^{s}&=&0,&\\
u^{s}(y)&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s).\end{array}\right.

We shall use Theorem
6.2
to determine the limit of
u^{s}(y)
as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty
. To that end we introduce the pair
(\tilde{u}^{s},\tilde{p}^{s})
solution of a similar problem set on the half-space
\mathbb{H}_{n}=\mathbb{H}_{n}(0)
. More precisely, we define

\tilde{u}^{s}(y)=u^{s}(y+sn),

\tilde{p}^{s}(y)=p^{s}(y+sn)
and consequently the pair
(\tilde{u}^{s},\tilde{p}^{s})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{y}\cdot A^{s}(y)\>\nabla_{y}\tilde{u}^{s}+\nabla_{y}\tilde{p}^{s}&=&0&\mbox{in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n},\\
\nabla_{y}\cdot\tilde{u}^{s}&=&0,&\\
\tilde{u}^{s}&=&g^{s}(y)&\mbox{on }\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

(6.16)

where
A^{s}(y):=A(y+sn)
and
g^{s}(y):=g(y+sn).
More generally, for a function
B
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we will use the notation
B^{s}(y)
to denote
B(y+sn).
We then have
\partial_{y_{\beta}}(B^{s}(y))=(\partial_{y_{\beta}}B)^{s}(y)
for all
\beta=1,...,d,
so one can simply denote it
\partial_{y_{\beta}}B^{s}(y)
without confusion.
An application of Proposition
5.12
yields :

Corollary 6.3
.

For all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, for all
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
and for all
\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
the following expansion of the Poisson kernel
P^{s}(y,\tilde{y})
associated to the system
(
6.16
)
holds

\displaystyle P^{s}(y,\tilde{y})=

\displaystyle-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\cdot n+\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n

\displaystyle-\{(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u^{*}_{bl})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot n

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\{(\pi^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})+(p_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\}\cdot\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes n

\displaystyle-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\left\{\left[(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot n

\displaystyle-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\left\{\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}[(\Gamma^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})+(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot n

\displaystyle+\{[(Q^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})+(\pi^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes n

\displaystyle-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(\Gamma^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(\chi^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y}):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\cdot n

\displaystyle+R(y,\tilde{y})

with the remainder
R(y,\tilde{y})
satisfying

\displaystyle|R(y,\tilde{y})|\leq\frac{C}{|\tilde{y}-y|^{d-1+\kappa}},\quad\mbox{ for all }\kappa\in(0,1/2d),

(6.17)

where the constant
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
M_{1}
.

Corollary 6.4
.

For arbitrary
s\in\mathbb{R}
we have the convergence of
u^{s}
towards a boundary layer tail :

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u^{s}(y)=U^{s,\infty}(n)

(6.18)

with

\displaystyle U_{i}^{s,\infty}(n)=

\displaystyle\>\>\mathcal{M}\left(\!-n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{j}(A^{s})^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g^{s}_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\!\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{l}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(\chi^{*})_{kj}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g^{s}_{r}(\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{l}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u_{bl}^{*})_{kj}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g^{s}_{r}(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.

Proof.

Injecting the above expansion of
P^{s}(y,\tilde{y})
in the representation of the solution
\tilde{u}^{s}

\displaystyle\tilde{u}^{s}(y)=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}P^{s}(y,\tilde{y})g^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

(6.19)

we obtain an expansion of
\tilde{u}^{s}
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\tilde{u}^{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\;U_{s}^{0,1}(y)+U_{s}^{0,2}(y)+U_{s}^{0,3}(y)+U_{s}^{0,4}(y)+U_{s}^{1,1}(y)+U_{s}^{1,2}(y)+U_{s}^{1,3}(y)

\displaystyle+U_{s}^{2,1}(y)+U_{s}^{2,2}(y)+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})g^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

(6.20)

where

\displaystyle U_{s}^{0,1}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y},\qquad U_{s}^{0,2}:=\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes ng^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{0,3}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\{\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]\nabla G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{0,4}(y):=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{(\pi^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})+(p_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\}\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\otimes ng^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

are the terms involving
\nabla G^{*,0},\,\Pi^{*,0}
. The remaining terms involve second and third order derivatives of
G^{*,0}
:

\displaystyle U_{s}^{1,1}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\left\{\left[(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{1,2}(y)=

\displaystyle\ \int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{[(Q^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})+(\pi^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)\}\otimes ng^{s}(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{1,3}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left\{-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})\nabla_{\!\tilde{y}}\left[(\Gamma^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)+(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right]:\nabla^{2}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{2,1}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left\{-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(\Gamma^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\right\}\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle U_{s}^{2,2}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\{-(A^{*})^{s}(\tilde{y})(\chi^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right)(u_{bl}^{*})^{s}\left(\tilde{y}\right):\nabla^{3}G^{*,0}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\}\cdot ng^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}.

The superscript
i
in
U_{s}^{i,j}
represents the order of the derivative of
\nabla G^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)
or
\Pi^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)
.

Remark that since
g^{s}
is bounded the remaining term
\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})g^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})g^{s}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(y\cdot n)^{\kappa}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Using the relation between
u^{s}
and
\tilde{u}^{s}
together with an application of Theorem
6.2
to the solution of problem (
6.16
) one obtains the convergence of
u^{s}
for arbitrary
s\in\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u^{s}(y)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{u}^{s}(y)=U^{s,\infty}(n)

(6.21)

with

\displaystyle U_{i}^{s,\infty}(n)=

\displaystyle\>\>\mathcal{M}\left(\!-n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{j}(A^{s})^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{j}g^{s}_{j}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\!\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\!\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\tilde{y},n\right)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{l}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(\chi^{*})_{kj}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g^{s}_{r}(\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\!\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\!\!\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\left[-\mathcal{M}(n_{\alpha}g^{s}_{l}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u^{*}_{bl})_{kj}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}})+\mathcal{M}\left(n_{r}g^{s}_{r}(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}|_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\right)\right]\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},n)d\tilde{y}.\qquad\qed

Now we compare two boundary layer tails
U^{s,\infty}(n)
and
U^{t,\infty}(n)
associated respectively to arbitrary
s,t\in\mathbb{R}.
We proceed through a study of the difference
U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)
.

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle|U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta}

with
C=C\left(\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

Proof.

The boundary layer tail
U^{s,\infty}(n)
can be seen as the limit of the expansion (
6.20
) as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty
and the difference
U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)
is then the limit of the difference of the correspponding expansions. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{u}^{s}(y)-\tilde{u}^{t}(y)

and using the expansion (
6.20
) this is written

\displaystyle U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}(U_{s}^{i,j}(y)-U_{t}^{i,j}(y))+\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})(g^{s}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}(\tilde{y}))\ d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}(U_{s}^{i,j}(y)-U_{t}^{i,j}(y))

since

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}R(y,\tilde{y})(g^{s}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}(\tilde{y}))\ d\tilde{y}=0.

Now we aim at establishing that for
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle|U^{i,j}_{s}(y)-U^{i,j}_{t}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s-t|,\quad\mbox{for some constant }C>0.

Step 1 : Bound of the difference
U_{s}^{0,1}-U^{0,1}_{t}

Consider the
i^{th}
component of this quantity

\displaystyle U^{0,1}_{s,i}(y)-U^{0,1}_{t,i}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}(A^{t})^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y})g^{t}_{j}(\tilde{y})\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}(A^{s})^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y})g^{s}_{j}(\tilde{y})\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y}+tn)g_{j}(\tilde{y}+tn)\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y}+sn)g_{j}(\tilde{y}+sn)\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)\,d\tilde{y}

The function
\tilde{y}\mapsto A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}(\tilde{y})g_{j}(\tilde{y})
is regular enough to satisfy

\displaystyle|A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}g_{j}(\tilde{y}+sn)-A^{\beta\alpha}_{kj}g_{j}(\tilde{y}+tn)|\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},\quad\mbox{ with }C=C(\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})>0.

We then obtain

\displaystyle|U^{0,1}_{s,i}(y)-U^{0,1}_{t,i}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ki}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)|\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and
\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Step 2 : Estimating the difference
U_{s}^{0,2}-U_{t}^{0,2}
:

The
i^{th}
component of this quantity reads

\displaystyle U_{s,i}^{0,2}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,2}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}g_{j}(\tilde{y}+sn)\,d\tilde{y}-\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}g_{j}(\tilde{y}+tn)\,d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{j}[g_{j}(\tilde{y}+sn)-g_{j}(\tilde{y}+tn)]\,d\tilde{y}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle|U_{s,i}^{0,2}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,2}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\,[g]_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}|s-t|^{\eta}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\Pi_{i}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)|\,d\tilde{y}.

By the fact that

\left\|\Pi^{*,0}_{i}\left(\cdot,y\right)\right\|_{L^{1}(\partial\mathbb{H}_{n})}\leq C(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}})
uniformly in
y

we get

\displaystyle|U_{s,i}^{0,2}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,2}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\,C|s-t|^{\eta}

with
C=C\left(d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},[g]_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

Step 3 : We treat the term
U_{s}^{0,3}-U_{t}^{0,3}
:

We write this difference into coordinates

\displaystyle U_{s,i}^{0,3}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,3}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(\chi^{*})^{s,\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g^{s}_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}(A^{t})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(\chi^{*})^{t,\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g^{t}_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-n_{\alpha}(A^{s})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u_{bl}^{*})^{s,\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g^{s}_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{\alpha}(A^{t})^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\left(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u_{bl}^{*})^{t,\beta}_{kj}\left(\tilde{y}\right)\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)g^{t}_{l}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

which is also written

\displaystyle U_{s,i}^{0,3}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,3}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-[B^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+sn\right)-B^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+tn\right)]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-[D^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+sn\right)-D^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+tn\right)]\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}\left(\tilde{y},y\right)d\tilde{y}

where
B_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y}):=n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}\chi_{kj}^{*,\beta}(\tilde{y})g_{l}(\tilde{y})
and
D_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y}):=n_{\alpha}A^{\gamma\alpha}_{kl}(\tilde{y})\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\gamma}}(u_{bl}^{*})^{\beta}_{kj}(\tilde{y})g_{l}(\tilde{y}).

These functions
B_{j}^{\beta}
and
D_{j}^{\beta}(\tilde{y})
satisfy

\displaystyle|B^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+sn\right)-B^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+tn\right)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+sn\right)-D^{\beta}_{j}\left(\tilde{y}+tn\right)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},

where
C>0
is a constant depending on
\mu
,
\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and on
\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|U_{s,i}^{0,3}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,3}(y)|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|s-t|^{\eta}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)|d\tilde{y},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C|s-t|^{\eta},

with
C=C(d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

Step 4 : Term
U_{s}^{0,4}-U_{t}^{0,4}
:

Consider the
i^{th}
-component of the difference
U_{s}^{0,4}(y)-U_{t}^{0,4}(y)

\displaystyle U_{s,i}^{0,4}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,4}(y)=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left((\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})+(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{r}g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\left((\pi^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})+(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)n_{r}g^{t}_{r}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{r}\left(g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}_{r}(\tilde{y})(\pi^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}n_{r}\left(g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}_{r}(\tilde{y})(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})\right)\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G_{ji}^{*,0}(\tilde{y},y)(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}.

The quantity
g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})
is Lipschitz continuous in
s

\displaystyle|g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(\pi^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}_{r}(\tilde{y})(\pi^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle=|g_{r}(\tilde{y}+sn)\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}(\tilde{y}+sn)-g_{r}(\tilde{y}+tn)\pi_{j}^{*,\beta}(\tilde{y}+tn)|

\displaystyle\leq\>C|s-t|^{\eta}

where
C=C(d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}).

The same inequality holds for
g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})
:

\displaystyle|g^{s}_{r}(\tilde{y})(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{s,\beta}(\tilde{y})-g^{t}_{r}(\tilde{y})(p_{bl}^{*})_{j}^{t,\beta}(\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\>C|s-t|^{\eta}.

Hence we get

\displaystyle|U_{s,i}^{0,4}(y)-U_{t,i}^{0,4}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta}\int_{\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{\beta}}G^{*,0}_{ji}(\tilde{y},y)|d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta}

with
C
depending on
d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and
\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

All the other terms in the expansion (
6.20
) converge to
0
as
y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty.

Putting together steps 1 through 4 one obtains

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{4}|U_{s}^{0,k}(y)-U_{t}^{0,k}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},\quad\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and
\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
This inequality implies

\displaystyle\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{4}|U_{s}^{0,k}(y)-U_{t}^{0,k}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|s-t|^{\eta},

and subsequently

\displaystyle|U^{s,\infty}(n)-U^{t,\infty}(n)|\leq

\displaystyle\>\sum_{i,j}\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}|U_{s}^{i,j}(y)-U_{t}^{i,j}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C|s-t|^{\eta}

where
C=C\left(d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right).

∎

A direct consequence of this Proposition is that the boundary layer tail
U^{\infty,s}(n)
is uniformly
continuous in
s\in\mathbb{R}.

Proposition 6.6
.

For
n\notin\mathbb{R}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle U^{t,\infty}(n)=U^{s,\infty}(n).

Proof.

Let
(u,p)
be the solution to the problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\!y}\cdot A(y)\nabla_{\!y}u+\nabla_{\!y}p&=&0&\mbox{in }\{y\cdot n>0\},\\
\nabla_{\!y}\cdot u&=&0,&\\
u(y)&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\{y\cdot n=0\}.\end{array}\right.

For arbitrary
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we set

u^{h}:=u(\cdot-h),\;p^{h}:=p(\cdot-h)
. Then, by the periodicity of
A=A(y)
and
g=g(y)
, the pair
(u^{h},p^{h})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\!y}\cdot A(y)\nabla_{\!y}u^{h}+\nabla_{\!y}p^{h}&=&0&\mbox{in }\{y\cdot n-h\cdot n>0\},\\
\nabla_{\!y}\cdot u^{h}&=&0,&\\
u^{h}(y)&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\{y\cdot n-h\cdot n=0\}.\end{array}\right.

The pair
(u^{h},p^{h})
coincides with the solution
(u^{s},p^{s})
to problem (
1.1
) with
s=h\cdot n
. Hence, by the definition of
U^{s,\infty}(n),
we have
\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u^{h}(y)=U^{s,\infty}(n),\;s=h\cdot n
.
Then we also have
\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u^{h}(y)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u(y-h)=\lim_{y\cdot n\rightarrow\infty}u(y)=U^{\infty}(n):=U^{0,\infty}(n)
. So we obtain the following equality on the boundary layer tails

U^{s,\infty}(n):=U^{\infty}(n),\quad\mbox{ for all }s\in\{h\cdot n:h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}.

Because
n\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
is irrational the set
\{h\cdot n:h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}
is a dense subset of
\mathbb{R}
. Now, the uniform continuity on
\mathbb{R}
of
s\mapsto U^{s,\infty}(n)
allows to conclude that
U^{s,\infty}(n):=U^{\infty}(n)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
.∎

Appendix A
Proof of some estimates

In what follows we provide some regularity estimates for solutions
u^{s}\in L^{\infty}
of the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\>\nabla u^{s}+\nabla p^{s}&=&0&\mbox{in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s),\\
\nabla\cdot u^{s}&=&0,&\\
u^{s}&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s),\end{array}\right.

(A.1)

satisfying the condition (
1.8
).

First, remark that if the vector
u^{s}
satisfies (
1.8
) then, for
q\in(1,\infty)
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{q}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}

\displaystyle=\int_{1}^{\infty}\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla u^{s}(M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))|^{q}dz_{d}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\delta^{q}}d\delta

\displaystyle\leq C,

(A.2)

where the constant
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

The bound (
1.8
) does not allow a control of the
L^{q}
-norm of
\nabla_{\!y}u^{s}
up to the boundary
\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s)
. However, one gains control of this derivative near the boundary by using the following local boundary Lipschitz estimate.

Lemma A.1
.

For arbitrary
\bar{y}\in\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s)
we define
D_{r}(\bar{y})
and
\Gamma_{r}(\bar{y})
as
D_{r}(\bar{y}):=B_{r}(\bar{y})\cap{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s)
and
\Gamma_{r}(\bar{y}):=B_{r}(\bar{y})\cap\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s)
. Then we have

\|\nabla_{\!y}u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{1}(\bar{y}))}\leq C<\infty

(A.3)

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\Gamma_{2}(\bar{y}))}
.

Proof.

A Lipschitz estimate given in
[
15
]
, Theorem 2.7, yields

\|\nabla_{y}\>u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{1}(\bar{y}))}\leq C\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D_{2}(\bar{y})}|u^{s}(y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{2}(\bar{y}))}+\|\nabla g\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\Gamma_{2}(\bar{y}))}\right\}.

Moreover, for
u^{s}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s))
there is a constant
C
with
0<C<\infty
such that

|u^{s}(y)|\leq C,\quad\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}(s),

which implies

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D_{2}(\bar{y})}|u^{s}(y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}\leq C.

Hence one has

\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{1}(\bar{y}))}\leq C

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,\eta
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s))}
.
∎

By Lemma
A.1
we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{1}(\bar{y}))}

\displaystyle\leq C,\qquad\mbox{ for all }\bar{y}\in\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s).

This fact implies

\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(\{0<y\cdot n-s<1\})}\leq C,

(A.4)

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,\eta
,
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
and
\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s))}
.

The following Lemma gives the
L^{q}
-boundedness of the derivative
\nabla u
in the normal direction uniformally in the tangential variable.

Lemma A.2
.

For
q\in(1,\infty)
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla u(M(z^{\prime},z_{d}))|^{q}dz_{d}\leq C<\infty,

where
C
depends on
d,\mu,\eta,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
,
\eta\in(0,1)
and
\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s))}
.

In particular, one has

\displaystyle M_{q}:=\left(\sup_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\int_{T_{h}}|\nabla u(Mz)|^{q}dz\right)^{\!\!\!1/q}<\infty,\quad T_{h}:=(0,1)^{d-1}+h,\ h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}.

(A.5)

Proof.

Using estimates (
A.2
) and (
A.4
) we find that there exists
C=C(d,\mu,\eta,[A]_{C^{0,\eta}},\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n}(s))})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{q}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}

\displaystyle\leq C<\infty.

(A.6)

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{q}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{q}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{1}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{q}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}C\left(\|g\|_{\infty}+\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))}\right)^{q}dz_{d}+C\|g\|^{q}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|g\|_{\infty}+\|g\|_{C^{1,\eta}(\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))}\right)^{q}+C\|g\|^{q}_{\infty}<\infty.\qed

Control of the second order derivative

Lemma A.3
.

For all
i=1,...,d,
one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{2}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}<\infty.

Proof.

We shall make use of the previous result through an application of the following Caccioppoli inequality.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an open subset. Suppose
(u^{s},p^{s})
satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\>\nabla u^{s}+\nabla p^{s}&=&\nabla\cdot F&\mbox{in }\Omega,\\
\nabla\cdot u^{s}&=&0&\mbox{in }\Omega.\\
\end{array}\right.

Then for all
y_{0}\in\Omega
such that
B(y_{0},3)\subset\Omega
one has

\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},2))}\leq C\left(\|u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},3))}+\|F\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},3))}\right).\\

By differentiating the first two equations of (
A.1
)
we see that the pair
(u^{s},p^{s})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\>\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}u^{s}+\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}p^{s}&=&\nabla\cdot((\partial_{y_{i}}A(y))\nabla u^{s})&\mbox{in }\mathbb{H}_{n}(s),\\
\nabla\cdot\partial_{y_{i}}u^{s}&=&0&\mbox{in }\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)\\
\end{array}\right.

(A.7)

for all
i=1,...,d
.

Applying Lemma
A.4
to system (
A.7
) with
y_{0}=kn
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\geq 3
, yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},2))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y_{i}}u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},3))}+\|\partial_{y_{i}}A(y)\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(y_{0},3))}\right)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(kn,2))}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|\nabla A\|_{\infty})\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(kn,3)}

\displaystyle\leq C(A)\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{2}(B(kn,3)}

(A.8)

Let
T:=\{\bar{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}:0\leq|\bar{y}|\leq 1\}
. Then we have the following covering of the channel

T\times(2,+\infty)

T\times(2,+\infty)\subset\bigcup^{\infty}_{k=3}B(kn,2)

and consequently we obtain the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{T\times(2,+\infty)}|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla_{y}u^{s}|^{2}\leq\sum^{\infty}_{k=3}\int_{B(kn,2)}|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla_{y}u^{s}|^{2}

then by (
A.8
) and (
A.6
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{T\times(2,+\infty)}|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla_{y}u^{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(A)\sum^{\infty}_{k=3}\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B(kn,3)}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{2}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{2}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}\leq C(A,g)<\infty.

On the other hand one applies Lemma
A.1
to show

\|\nabla_{y}u^{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(T\times(0,2))}\leq C(g),

(A.9)

and subsequetly

\displaystyle\int_{T\times(0,+\infty)}|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla_{y}u^{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(A,g)<\infty.

This estimate holds also when
T
is replaced with
\tilde{y}+T
for all
\tilde{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)
. Then we arrive at

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\partial_{y_{i}}\nabla u^{s}(M(\cdot,z_{d}))\|^{2}_{L_{z^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}dz_{d}\leq C(A,g)<\infty,\quad\mbox{for all }i=1,...,d.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qed

Lemma A.5
.

Let
r>0
. For
q\in(1,\infty)
and for a pair
(u^{s},p^{s})
satisfying the first two equations of system
(
1.1
)
we have

\|p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{r}}\|_{L^{q}(D_{r})}\leq C\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{q}(D_{r})},

(A.10)

where
C
depends
d
and
\mu

Proof.

By a rescaling argument one can assume
r=1
without loss of generality.
Let
q^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
be such that
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
and let
\varphi\in L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})
. By the solvability of the divergence equation, see
[
1
]
(Theorem 4.1), there exists
\psi\in W_{0}^{1,q^{\prime}}(D_{1})
satisfying

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\psi=\varphi-(\varphi)_{D_{1}},\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\|\psi\|_{W_{0}^{1,q^{\prime}}(D_{1})}\leq C\|\varphi-(\varphi)_{D_{1}}\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})}

(A.11)

where the constant
C
depends on
d.

We then get

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\varphi

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})(\varphi-(\varphi)_{D_{1}})

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\nabla\cdot\psi

and integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\varphi

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}}-\nabla p^{s}\cdot\psi.

Now one uses the first equation in (
1.1
) and integrate by parts again

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\varphi

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}}[-\nabla\cdot A(y)\nabla u^{s}]\cdot\psi

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}}A(y)\nabla u^{s}\cdot\nabla\psi.

The Hölder inequality then yields

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\varphi

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{D_{1}}|\nabla u^{s}|\,|\nabla\psi|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{q}(D_{1})}\,\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})}.

Now one combines the latter inequality with the estimate (
A.11
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{D_{1}}(p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}})\varphi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{q}(D_{1})}\,\|\varphi-(\varphi)_{D_{1}}\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{q}(D_{1})}\,\|\varphi\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})}.

Seeing that
\varphi
is an arbitrary element of
L^{q^{\prime}}(D_{1})
one draws from the last estimate that

\displaystyle\|p^{s}-(p^{s})_{D_{1}}\|_{L^{q}(D_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u^{s}\|_{L^{q}(D_{1})}.\qed

(A.12)

We also have the following lemma that will be applied in the proof of the uniqueness of the solution to problem (
1.1
).

Lemma A.6
.

Let the pair
\left(u,p\right)
satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\nabla u+\nabla p&=&0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{H}_{n},\\
\nabla\cdot u&=&0.\end{array}\right.

Then for
r>0
and
q>1
we have the following estimates :

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}(D_{r}(0))}\leq M_{q}^{q}\;r^{d-1},

(A.13)

and

\displaystyle\|p\|^{q}_{L^{q}(D_{r}(0))}\leq Cr^{d-1},

(A.14)

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
,
M_{q}
and
q
. The constant
M_{q}
is defined in
(
A.5
)
.

Proof of
Lemma
A.6
.

Step 1 : Control of the velocity
\nabla u

We set
D_{1}(0):=\{z\in\mathbb{H}_{n}:|z|<1\}
and
D_{r}(0):=rD_{1}(0)
.
We shall use the following fact : for all
k\in\mathbb{N},
one has

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}(2^{k}D_{r}(0))}\leq N_{k}\cdot M^{q}_{q}

(A.15)

where
N_{k}
is the minimum number of cells
T_{h}
that it takes to cover
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\cap\overline{2^{k}D_{r}(0)}
. Therefore, the number
N_{k}
is at the order of the volume of
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\cap\overline{2^{k}D_{r}(0)}
since
|T_{h}|=1
. More precisely we have

\displaystyle N_{k}=O\left((2^{k}r)^{d-1}\right).

(A.16)

Thus, taking
k=0
in estimates (
A.15
) and (
A.16
) yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}(D_{r}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq M^{q}_{q}\;r^{d-1}.

Step 2 : Control of the pressure
p
:

We show that the limit
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}
exists by showing that the series

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sum^{N}_{k=0}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}=\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}

converges. From the identity

\displaystyle(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D(0,2^{k}r)}\left(p(y)-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right)dy

one gets (using Jensen’s inequality for instance)

\displaystyle\left|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D(0,2^{k}r)}\left|p(y)-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|^{q}dy\right)^{\!\!1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2^{dk}r^{d}|D_{1}(0)|}\int_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\left|p(y)-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|^{q}dy\right)^{\!\!1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\frac{1}{2^{dk/q}\,r^{d/q}}\left(\int_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\left|p(y)-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|^{q}dy\right)^{\!\!1/q}.

Then by the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|p(y)-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right\|_{L^{q}\left({D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right)}\leq C(d,\mu)\|\nabla u\|_{L^{q}\left(D(0,2^{k+1}r)\right)}

we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\frac{1}{2^{dk/q}}\frac{1}{r^{d/q}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{q}\left(D(0,2^{k+1}r)\right)}.

Using (
A.15
) together with (
A.16
) we control the quantity
\|\nabla u\|_{L^{q}(D(0,2^{k+1}r))}
as follows

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}(D(0,2^{k+1}r))}\leq

\displaystyle\ M^{q}_{q}(2^{k+1}r)^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ M^{q}_{q}\cdot 2^{(k+1)(d-1)}r^{d-1}.

We integrate this fact in the preceding inequality which yields

\displaystyle\left|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq M_{q}\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\frac{1}{2^{dk/q}}\frac{1}{r^{d/q}}\,2^{(k+1)(d-1)/q}\,r^{(d-1)/q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(d-1)/q}M_{q}\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\frac{2^{(d-1)k/q}}{2^{dk/q}}\,r^{-1/q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(d-1)/q}M_{q}\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\frac{1}{2^{k/q}}\,r^{-1/q}.

(A.17)

The estimate (
A.17
) yields

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(d-1)/q}M_{q}\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D_{1}(0)|^{1/q}}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left(\frac{1}{2^{1/q}}\right)^{k}r^{-1/q}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-1/q},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
M_{q}
. The latter estimate shows that the series

\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}

is absolutely convergent which implies that the series itself converges. As a consequence the quantity
(p)_{\infty}:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}
is well defined. One can assume that
(p)_{\infty}=0
since the pressure
p
is defined up to a constant. Indeed, if we make the switch
p\rightarrow p-(p)_{\infty}
then the limit becomes

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(p-(p)_{\infty}\right)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}=

\displaystyle\,\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>(p)_{\infty}-(p)_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>0.

Therefore the quantity
p
is also written

\displaystyle p=

\displaystyle\,p-(p)_{D(0,r)}+\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}

(A.18)

which implies

\displaystyle\|p\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}\leq

\displaystyle\,\|p-(p)_{D(0,r)}\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}+\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}.

(A.19)

The estimate (
2.1
) yields

\displaystyle\|p-(p)_{D(0,r)}\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,\mu)\|\nabla u\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,\mu)M_{q}\,r^{d/q}r^{-1/q}.

Now let us show that

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left\|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}\leq C(d,\mu)M_{q}\,r^{d/q}r^{-1/q}

with
C
depending on
d,\mu
and
M_{q}
. The estimate (
A.17
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(d-1)/q}M_{q}\cdot\frac{C(d,\mu)}{|D(0,1)|^{1/q}}\frac{1}{2^{k/q}}\,r^{-1/q}\cdot|D(0,r)|^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq M_{q}\cdot C(d,\mu,q)\ \frac{1}{2^{k/q}}\,r^{-1/q}r^{d/q}.

Then, by summing over
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\left\|(p)_{D(0,2^{k}r)}-(p)_{D(0,2^{k+1}r)}\right\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}

\displaystyle\leq M_{q}\cdot C(d,\mu,q)\left(\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\frac{1}{2^{k/q}}\right)\,\,r^{-1/q}r^{d/q}

\displaystyle\leq C(M_{q},d,\mu,q)\,r^{d/q}r^{-1/q}.

Hence by the inequality (
A.19
) we get

\displaystyle\|p\|_{L^{q}(D(0,r))}\ \leq\;

\displaystyle C(M_{q},d,\mu,q)r^{d/q}r^{-1/q}.\qed

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
2.8

Proof of
Proposition
2.8
.

The proof is divided into several steps.

Step 1 : Proof of the estimate

|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-2}}.

Let
y,\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
.
Set
r:=|y-\tilde{y}|
and let
(u,p)
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\>\nabla u+\nabla p&=&f&\mbox{in }{\mathbb{H}}_{n},\\
\nabla\cdot u&=&0,&\\
u&=&0&\mbox{on }\partial{\mathbb{H}}_{n},\end{array}\right.

(B.1)

where
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B(\tilde{y},r/3))
.

Then the velocity
u
admits the following representation

\displaystyle u(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}G(y,z)\cdot f(z)dz

(B.2)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}G(y,z)\cdot f(z)dz.

(B.3)

From the fact that
f
vanishes on
D_{r/3}(y)
one draws that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\!z}\cdot A(z)\nabla_{\!z}u(z)+\nabla_{\!z}p(z)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r/3}(y),\\
\nabla_{\!z}\cdot u(z)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r/3}(y),\\
u(z)&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r/3}(y).\end{array}\right.

(B.4)

Then, by a rescaled version of Lemma
2.5
there exists a constant
C
depending on
d,\mu,\|A\|_{C^{0,\eta}}
, such that, for
\lambda\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle[u]_{C^{0,\lambda}(D_{r/6}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{r^{d/2+\lambda}}\|u\|_{L^{2}(D_{r/3}(y))}.

(B.5)

If
z_{1},z_{2}\in D_{r/6}(y)
then
|z_{1}-z_{2}|\leq r/3
and
3^{\lambda}\frac{|u(z_{1})-u(z_{2})|}{r^{\lambda}}\leq\frac{|u(z_{1})-u(z_{2})|}{|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{\lambda}}
. Hence, the estimate (
B.5
) leads to

\displaystyle|u(y)|\leq C\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D(y,r/3)}|u(z)|^{2}dz\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}.

Rewriting the latter inequality with the representation formula (
6.1
)
one obtains

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}G(y,z)\cdot f(z)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(y,r/3)}|u(z)|^{2}dz\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(y,r/3)}|u(z)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dz\right)^{\!\!\!\frac{d-2}{2d}}\mbox{ by H\"{o}lder inequality}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{r^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}|u(z)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dz\right)^{\!\!\!\frac{d-2}{2d}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{r^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}|u(z)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}dz\right)^{\!\!\!\frac{d-2}{2d}}.

We then use the Sobolev inequality to get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}G(y,z)\cdot f(z)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}|\nabla u(z)|^{2}dz\right)^{\!\!\!\frac{1}{2}}

which, combined with the energy estimate

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq Cr\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{n})},

yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}G(y,z)\cdot f(z)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cr}{r^{\frac{d}{2}-1}}\;\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{n})}

and by duality this inequality gives

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}|G(y,z)|^{2}dz\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cr}{r^{\frac{d}{2}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cr^{2}}{r^{d/2}}.

(B.6)

Now one multiplies both sides of (
B.6
) by
\frac{1}{r^{d/2}}

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}|G(y,z)|^{2}dz\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{r^{d-2}}.

(B.7)

By applying Lemma
2.5
to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{z}\cdot A^{*}(z)\nabla_{z}G^{*}(z,y)+\nabla_{z}\Pi^{*}(z,y)&=&0&\mbox{in }\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3),\\
\nabla_{z}\cdot G^{*}(z,y)&=&0&\mbox{in }\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3),\\
G^{*}(z,y)&=&0&\mbox{on }\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3).\end{array}\right.

we obtain

\displaystyle|G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq C\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}\cap B(\tilde{y},r/3)}|G^{*}(z,y)|^{2}dz\right)^{\!\!\!1/2}.

(B.8)

Now the estimates (
B.7
) and (
B.8
) together give

\displaystyle|G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq C\frac{1}{r^{d-2}}

Then by the symmetry property (
2.14
) we get

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{1}{r^{d-2}}.

Step 2 : Proof of the estimates

|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}
and
|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

Let us set
r:=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|.

We apply a rescaled version of Theorem
2.6
to the system of equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{z}\cdot A(z)\nabla_{z}G(z,\tilde{y})+\nabla_{z}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
\nabla_{z}\cdot G(\cdot,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
G(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r}(y).\end{array}\right.

(B.9)

to obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}.

(B.10)

From (
2.17
) one draws

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}=

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{D_{r}(y)}}|G(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{D_{r}(y)}}\frac{C}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-2}}

and, since

r=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|\mbox{ implies }|z-\tilde{y}|\geq r/2\mbox{ for all }z\in{D_{r}(y)},

we have

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{d-2}}.

Then by the inequality (
B.10
) one obtains

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\frac{1}{r^{d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{d-1}}

(B.11)

that is to say

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}},

where
C
depends on
d,\mu
and
[A]_{C^{0,\eta}}
.

As for the proof of the estimate
|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}
, we first apply the preceding argument to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot A^{*}(\tilde{y})\>\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)+\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\Pi^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(\tilde{y}),\\
\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(\tilde{y}),\\
G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r}(\tilde{y})\end{array}\right.

(B.12)

to obtain

|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

Then the symmetry property (
2.14
) yields

|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|=|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{T}\!(y,\tilde{y})|=|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

(B.13)

Step 3 : Proof of the estimates

|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}
and
|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

In this proof we set
r:=|y-\tilde{y}|.

First, we suppose
y\cdot n\geq\frac{r}{3}
. Then by the estimate

|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-2}}

we have

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq 3\;\frac{y\cdot n}{r}\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\;C\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

Now we assume that
y\cdot n<\frac{r}{3}
and let
\bar{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(0)
be such that
|y-\bar{y}|=y\cdot n
.
The mean value inequality yields

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})-G(\bar{y},\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D(\bar{y},\frac{r}{3})}\;|y-\bar{y}|.

Then by (
2.18
) and the fact that
G(\bar{y},\tilde{y})=0
, we get

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in D(\bar{y},\frac{r}{3})}\frac{C}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}\;y\cdot n.

(B.14)

Moreover one has

|z-\tilde{y}|\geq\frac{r}{3}\mbox{ for all }z\in{D(\bar{y},r/3)}.

Then estimate (
B.14
) gives

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{y\cdot n}{r^{d-1}},

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}.

As for the other estimate
we apply the same reasoning right above by
interchanging
y
and
\tilde{y},
and using the mean value inequality along with (
2.19
).

Step 4 : Proof of the estimates

|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}
and
|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

Let
r:=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|.

A rescaled version of Theorem
2.6
applied to the system of equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{z}\cdot A(z)\nabla_{z}G(z,\tilde{y})+\nabla_{z}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
\nabla_{z}\cdot G(\cdot,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
G(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r}(y).\end{array}\right.

(B.15)

yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}.

(B.16)

From (
2.17
) one draws

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}=

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in{D_{r}(y)}}|G(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\sup_{z\in{D_{r}(y)}}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}

and, since

r=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|\mbox{ implies }|z-\tilde{y}|\geq r/2\mbox{ for all }z\in{D_{r}(y)},

we have

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d-1}C\;\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d-1}}.

Then by the estimate (
B.16
) one obtains

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d-2}C\;\frac{1}{r}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\;\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d}}

and one replaces
r
by
\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|
to obtain

|\nabla_{y}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\;\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.\\

(B.17)

To prove the other estimate, we first apply the the preceding estimate to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot A^{*}(\tilde{y})\>\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)+\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\Pi^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(\tilde{y}),\\
\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\cdot G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(\tilde{y}),\\
G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r}(\tilde{y}),\end{array}\right.

(B.18)

to get

|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq C\;\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

We then use the symmetry (
2.14
) to obtain

|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|=|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{T}\!(y,\tilde{y})|=|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G^{*}(\tilde{y},y)|\leq C\;\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.\\

Step 5 : Proof of the estimate

|G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{(y\cdot n)(\tilde{y}\cdot n)}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

We apply again the reasoning carried out in Step 3.
Let
r:=|y-\tilde{y}|.

First, we suppose
y\cdot n\geq\frac{r}{3}
. Then by the estimate (
2.17
) we have

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq 3\;\frac{y\cdot n}{r}\left(C\,\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;C\,\frac{(y\cdot n)(\tilde{y}\cdot n)}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

Now we assume that
y\cdot n<\frac{r}{3}
and let
\bar{y}\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(0)
be such that
|y-\bar{y}|=y\cdot n
.
The mean value inequality yields

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})-G(\bar{y},\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{y}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D(\bar{y},\frac{r}{3})}\;|y-\bar{y}|.

Therefore, by (
2.18
) together with
G(\bar{y},\tilde{y})=0
, we get

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{z\in D(\bar{y},\frac{r}{3})}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\;y\cdot n,

which leads to

\displaystyle|G(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{(y\cdot n)(\tilde{y}\cdot n)}{r^{d}},

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{(y\cdot n)(\tilde{y}\cdot n)}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

Step 6 : Proof of the estimate

|\nabla_{y}\nabla_{\tilde{y}}G(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\frac{(y\cdot n)(\tilde{y}\cdot n)}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

Let
r:=\frac{2}{3}|y-\tilde{y}|.

First, one differentiates the system (
B.15
) with respect to the variable
\tilde{y}_{k}
,
k=1,...,d
, to obtain

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{y}\cdot A(y)\>\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y}))+\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(y,\tilde{y}))&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
\nabla_{y}\cdot\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }D_{r}(y),\\
\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{on }\Gamma_{r}(y).\end{array}\right.

(B.19)

Then Theorem
2.6
applied to this system yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y}))\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{D_{r}(y)}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(z,\tilde{y})|^{2}dz\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r}\|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}.

(B.20)

Furthermore, by the estimate (
B.13
), one has

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r}(y))}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in{D_{r}}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in{D_{r}}}\frac{C}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{d-1}}.

(B.21)

The inequalities (
B.20
) and (
B.21
) jointly give

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y}))\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{d}}

hence

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y}))\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{r/2}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}.

Step 7 : Proof of the estimates

|\Pi(y,\tilde{y})-\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|\leq C\min\;\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}},\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\}.

Let
\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
be fixed and let
y,z\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
be such that
|y-\tilde{y}|\leq|z-\tilde{y}|
. We set
r:=|y-\tilde{y}|
and consider the annuli
A_{k}:=D(\tilde{y},2^{k}r)\char 92\relax\overline{D(\tilde{y},2^{k-1}r)},\,k=1,2,..
. For each
k
we find a covering of
\overline{A}_{k}
as follows. For instance, for
k=1
there is a covering
(B_{1j})_{j=1,...,N}
of
\overline{A}_{1}
where, for all
j=1,...,N
,

B_{1j}
is a ball satisfying
\diam B_{1j}=2r
and
\dist(\tilde{y},B_{1j})\geq 2^{-1}r
. Then, through the map
\sigma_{k}:y\mapsto 2^{k-1}(y-\tilde{y})+\tilde{y}
one can see that the balls
B_{kj}=\sigma_{k}(B_{1j}),j=1,...,N
form a covering of
\overline{A}_{k}
and satisfy
\diam B_{kj}=2^{k}r
and
\dist(\tilde{y},B_{kj})\geq 2^{k-2}r
. Moreover, the smallest finite number
N
that can be taken depend only on
d
.
Now one applies Theorem
2.6
to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{z}\cdot A(z)\nabla_{z}G(z,\tilde{y})+\nabla_{z}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }(5/4)B_{kj},\\
\nabla_{z}\cdot G(\cdot,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{in }(5/4)B_{kj},\\
G(z,\tilde{y})&=&0&\mbox{on }(5/4)B_{kj}\cap\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}\end{array}\right.

(B.22)

to obtain

\displaystyle\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}r}\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}.

(B.23)

Here,
(5/4)B_{kj}
denotes the ball that is
5/4
times larger than
B_{kj}
and with the same center. The inequality
\dist(\tilde{y},B_{kj})\geq 2^{k-2}r
then gives
\dist(\tilde{y},(5/4)B_{kj})\geq 2^{k-3}r
.
From (
2.17
) one draws

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}}}|G(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\sup_{z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}}}\frac{1}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-2}};\sup_{z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}}}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}}\right\}

and, since

\dist(\tilde{y},(5/4)B_{kj})\geq 2^{k-3}r\mbox{ implies }|z-\tilde{y}|\geq 2^{k-3}r\mbox{ for all }z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}},

we have

\displaystyle\|G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{C}{2^{k(d-2)}r^{d-2}},\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{2^{k(d-1)}r^{d-1}}\right\}.

Then by (
B.23
) we obtain

\displaystyle\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}r}\min\left\{\frac{1}{2^{k(d-2)}r^{d-2}};\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{2^{k(d-1)}r^{d-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{2^{k(d-1)}}\frac{1}{r^{d-1}};\frac{1}{2^{kd}}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d}}\right\}.

From the fact

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})\subseteq\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\overline{A_{k}}\subseteq\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\left(\bigcup^{N}_{j=1}B_{kj}\right)

one draws

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})}{\textup{osc}}|\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\sum^{N}_{j=1}\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|
