\displaystyle\leq C\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\sum^{N}_{j=1}\min\left\{\frac{1}{2^{k(d-1)}}\frac{1}{r^{d-1}};\frac{1}{2^{kd}}\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq CN\min\left\{\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\left(\frac{1}{2^{d-1}}\right)^{k}\!\!\frac{1}{r^{d-1}},\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\left(\frac{1}{2^{d}}\right)^{k}\!\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq CN\min\left\{\frac{1}{r^{d-1}},\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{r^{d}}\right\},

that is to say

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})}{\textup{osc}}|\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}};\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\}.

This estimate clearly implies

\displaystyle|\Pi(y,\tilde{y})-\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}};\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\},\quad\mbox{ for all }z\in\mathbb{H}_{n},|z-\tilde{y}|\geq|y-\tilde{y}|.

Proof of the estimates

|\Pi(y,\tilde{y})|\leq C\min\;\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}},\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\}.

By using arguments similar to those in
[
17
]
(Appendix B) one obtains

|\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|\xrightarrow[z\cdot n>2]{|z|\rightarrow\infty}0.

Then, by taking the limits in estimates (
2.21
) as
|z|\xrightarrow[z\cdot n>2]{}\infty
one gets

\displaystyle|\Pi(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}};\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\}.

Step 8 : Proof of the estimates

|\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\Pi(y,\tilde{y})-\nabla_{\tilde{y}}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|\leq\;C\min\;\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}};\frac{\tilde{y}\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d+1}}\right\}.

Again we set
r:=|y-\tilde{y}|
and consider the annuli
A_{k}:=D(\tilde{y},2^{k}r)\char 92\relax D(\tilde{y},2^{k-1}r),\,k=1,2,...

and the covering
(B_{kj})_{j=1,...,N}
of
\overline{A}_{k}
defined in the previous step. We apply Theorem
2.6
to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{y}\cdot A(y)\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y}))+\nabla_{y}(\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(y,\tilde{y}))&=&0\ \quad\mbox{in }(5/4)B_{kj}\\
\nabla_{y}\cdot\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y})&=&0\;\quad\mbox{in }(5/4)B_{kj},\\
\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(y,\tilde{y})&=&0\;\quad\mbox{on }(5/4)B_{kj}\cap\partial\mathbb{H}_{n},\end{array}\right.

(B.24)

to obtain

\displaystyle\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}r}\|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}

(B.25)

From estimates (
2.19
) one draws

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\sup_{z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}}}\frac{1}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d-1}};\sup_{z\in{(5/4)B_{kj}}}\frac{y\cdot n}{|z-\tilde{y}|^{d}}\right\}

which leads to

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}G(\cdot,\tilde{y})\|_{L^{\infty}((5/4)B_{kj})}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{C}{2^{k(d-1)}r^{d-1}},\frac{y\cdot n}{2^{kd}r^{d}}\right\}.

Then by (
B.25
) we obtain

\displaystyle\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{2^{kd}}\frac{1}{r^{d}};\frac{1}{2^{k(d+1)}}\frac{y\cdot n}{r^{d+1}}\right\}.

From the fact

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})\subset\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\overline{A}_{k}\subset\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\left(\bigcup^{N}_{j=1}B_{kj}\right)

one draws

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})}{\textup{osc}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\sum^{N}_{j=1}\underset{B_{kj}}{\textup{osc}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\sum^{N}_{j=1}\min\left\{\frac{1}{2^{kd}}\frac{1}{r^{d}};\frac{1}{2^{k(d+1)}}\frac{y\cdot n}{r^{d+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq CN\min\left\{\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\left(\frac{1}{2^{d}}\right)^{k}\frac{1}{r^{d}};\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}\left(\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}\right)^{k}\frac{y\cdot n}{r^{d+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq CN\min\left\{\frac{1}{r^{d}};\frac{y\cdot n}{r^{d+1}}\right\}

that is to say

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbb{H}_{n}\char 92\relax D_{r}(\tilde{y})}{\textup{osc}}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(\cdot,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}};\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d+1}}\right\}.

This estimate implies

\displaystyle|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(y,\tilde{y})-\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}};\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d+1}}\right\},\quad\mbox{ for all }z\in\mathbb{H}_{n},|z-\tilde{y}|\geq|y-\tilde{y}|.

Now, we take the limit as
|z|\rightarrow\infty
and use the fact
\lim_{|z|\rightarrow\infty}|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(z,\tilde{y})|=0
in the latter estimate to get

\displaystyle|\partial_{\tilde{y}_{k}}\Pi(y,\tilde{y})|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\left\{\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d}};\frac{y\cdot n}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{d+1}}\right\}.\qed

Appendix C
Uniqueness in Theorem
1.1

Here we give a proof of the uniqueness of the solution
u^{s}
to problem (
1.1
) under hypothesis (
1.8
). Once the uniqueness property is satisfied the solution to problem such as (
1.1
) can be represented by an integral formula e.g. through Poisson’s kernel.

Proof of the uniqueness of the solution to problem
(
1.1
)
.

If
(u^{s}_{1},p^{s}_{1})
and
(u^{s}_{2},p^{s}_{2})
are two solutions of (
1.1
) then the pair
(u,p)
,
u:=u_{1}^{s}-u^{s}_{2},\,p:=p_{1}^{s}-p_{2}^{s},
satisfies
the problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\nabla\cdot A(y)\nabla u+\nabla p&=&0\;\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{H}_{n}(s),\\
\nabla\cdot u&=&0,\\
u&=&0\quad\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(s).\end{array}\right.

(C.1)

We intend to show that
u=0
by showing that for any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))
one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}u(y)\cdot f(y)dy=0.

(C.2)

So let
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))
and consider the pair
(V,S)
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(y)\nabla V+\>\nabla S&=&f\;\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{H}_{n}(s),\\
\nabla\cdot V&=&0,\\
V&=&0\quad\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}(s).\end{array}\right.

(C.3)

The solution
V
admits a representation formula through Green’s kernel
G^{*}
associated with the operator
-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(y)\nabla
and the domain
\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)

\displaystyle V(y)=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}G^{*}(y,\tilde{y})f(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}.

(C.4)

We claim that
u
, solution to problem (
C.1
), satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f(y)\cdot u(y)dy=0.

(C.5)

Indeed, by carrying out integrations by parts one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f(y)\cdot u(y)dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f^{i}(y)u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}[-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)+\>\partial_{y_{i}}S(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)\partial_{y_{\alpha}}u^{i}(y)dy-\>\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}S(y)\partial_{y_{i}}u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}A^{*}(y)\nabla V\cdot\nabla u\,dy+\>\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}-S\,(\nabla\cdot\ \!u)dy.

The integrations by parts that we perform here are justified below.
Now, by using the second equation in (
C.1
) the last line reduces to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f(y)\cdot u(y)dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}\nabla u\cdot A^{*}(y)\nabla Vdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}A(y)\nabla u\cdot\nabla Vdy.

We integrate by parts again and use the first equation in (
C.1
)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f^{i}(y)u^{i}(y)dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}(-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y))\,V^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}-\partial_{i}p(y)V^{i}(y)dy.

Another integration by parts yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}-\partial_{i}p(y)V^{i}(y)dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}p(y)\partial_{i}V^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}p\,(\nabla\cdot V)dy

Then we use the second equation in (
C.3
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}p\,(\nabla\cdot V)dy=0.

This proves that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}(s)}f(y)\cdot u(y)dy=0

for arbitrary
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))
. Hence
u=0
.
∎

Next, we set forth a justification of the integrations by parts used in the above proof of the uniqueness of
u^{s}
.

Without loss of generality, we set
s=0
.

Integration by parts of the term
\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}[-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy
.

We use the following equality

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}[-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy=\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}[-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy

to reduce the integration over an unbounded domain to an integration over a bounded domain. Now we carry out integrations by parts for the integral in the right-hand-side :

\displaystyle\int_{D(0,R)}

\displaystyle[-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{D(0,R)}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}u^{i}(y)+\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy

where
e^{\alpha}
is the
\alpha^{th}
component of the unit exterior normal
e
of
\partial D(0,R)
. Then taking the limits as
R\rightarrow\infty
we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}u^{i}(y)dy=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}u^{i}(y)dy

provided

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy=0.

(C.6)

We will show that (
C.6
) holds below.

Integration by parts of the term
\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}\partial_{y_{i}}S(y)\,u^{i}(y)dy
.

Similarly, for the term involving the pressure
S
one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}[\partial_{y_{i}}S(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}[\partial_{y_{i}}S(y)]\,u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\left(\int_{D(0,R)}-S(y)\partial_{y_{i}}u^{i}(y)dy+\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}-S(y)\partial_{y_{i}}u^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}-S(y)\partial_{y_{i}}u^{i}(y)dy

as long as

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy=0.

(C.7)

We will show that (
C.7
) holds below.

Integration by parts of the term
\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy
.

On the one hand we have the limiting equality

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}

\displaystyle A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy=\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy

and on the other hand we have the integration by parts for the integral in the right hand side

\displaystyle\int_{D(0,R)}

\displaystyle A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{D(0,R)}(-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y))\,V^{i}(y)dy+\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy.

Now one lets
R\rightarrow\infty
in the preceding equality to obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)\,\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!V^{i}(y)dy=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}(-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y))\,V^{i}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}(-\partial_{y_{\alpha}}\!A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y))\,V^{i}(y)dy

as long as

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy=0.\\

We will show that (
C
) holds below.

Integration by parts of the term
\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}-\partial_{i}p(y)V^{i}(y)dy
.

For this term involving a pressure, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}-\partial_{i}p(y)V^{i}(y)dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}-\nabla p\cdot Vdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R)}-\nabla p\cdot Vdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\left(\int_{D(0,R)}p\,(\nabla\cdot V)dy-\int_{\partial D(0,R)}p(y)\,V(y)\cdot e\,dy\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}p(y)\,(\nabla\cdot V(y))dy

as long as

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}p(y)\,V(y)\cdot e\,dy=0

(C.8)

where the vector
e
is the unit exterior normal of
\partial D(0,R)
.
We will show that (
C.8
) holds below.

Now we prove that the integrals over the boundary
\partial D(0,R)
in (
C.6
)-(
C.8
) vanish as
R\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let

f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{H}_{n})
and let the pair
(V,S)
be a solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\nabla\cdot A^{*}(y)\nabla V+\>\nabla S&=&f\;\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{H}_{n},\\
\nabla\cdot V&=&0,\\
V&=&0\quad\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{H}_{n},\end{array}\right.

(C.9)

then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}
and
|y|
large enough, we have

\displaystyle|V(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{y\cdot n}{|y|^{d}},

(C.10)

\displaystyle|\nabla V(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{|y|^{d}},

(C.11)

\displaystyle|S(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{|y|^{d}}.

(C.12)

Step 1 : proof of (
C.6
)

Seeing that
u=0
on
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy=C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy

which yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|u(y)|\cdot|\nabla V(y)|dy.

From
u:=u_{1}^{s}-u_{2}^{s}
with
u_{1}^{s},u_{2}^{s}
belonging to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{n}(s))
, we get

\displaystyle|u(y)|

\displaystyle\leq|u^{s}_{1}(y)|+|u^{s}_{2}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C,\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n},

(C.13)

and from
estimate (
C.11
) we draw

\displaystyle|\nabla V(y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{d}},\qquad\qquad\mbox{ for }y\in\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n},

where
C>0
is independent of
R
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{1}{R^{d}}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\,\frac{1}{R^{d}}C(d)R^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{R},

where
C
is independent of
R.
This implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}-u^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}(A^{*})^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}V^{j}(y)dy\right|\xrightarrow{R\rightarrow\infty}0.

Step 2 : proof of (
C.7
)

Given that
u=0
on
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
one has the equality

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy\right|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|S(y)||u(y)|dy.

Then by using estimates (
C.12
) and (
C.13
) one obtains

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy\right|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{1}{R^{d}}dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\frac{1}{R^{d}}\cdot R^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\,\frac{1}{R}

hence

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\partial D(0,R)}S(y)u^{i}(y)e^{i}dy=

\displaystyle 0.

Step 3 : proof of (
C
)
.

Because
V=0
on
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
we have the equality

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy=\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}|V(y)|\cdot|\nabla u(y)|dy.

As
u^{s}_{1}
and
u^{s}_{2}
satisfy (
1.8
) one has

\displaystyle|\nabla u(y)|

\displaystyle\leq|\nabla u^{s}_{1}(y)|+|\nabla u^{s}_{2}(y)|,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{y\cdot n},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{ for all }y\in\mathbb{H}_{n}.

Therefore, by using estimate (
C.10
) one gets

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R)}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{1}{y\cdot n}\frac{y\cdot n}{|y|^{d}}dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{1}{|y|^{d}}dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}\frac{1}{R^{d}}dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{R},

where
C
is independent of
R
. Which yields

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R)\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}V^{i}(y)e^{\alpha}A^{\alpha\beta}_{ij}(y)\partial_{y_{\beta}}u^{j}(y)dy\xrightarrow{R\rightarrow\infty}0.

Step 4 : proof of (
C.8
)
.

The estimate (
A.14
) gives : for
q>1,

\displaystyle\int_{D(0,R)}|p(y)|^{q}dy\leq\;

\displaystyle C(M_{q},d,\mu)R^{d-1},\quad\mbox{ for all }R\geq 1,

which yields a sequence
(R_{k})_{k}
of positive numbers satisfying
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}R_{k}=\infty
and

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}|p(y)|^{q}dy\leq CR_{k}^{d-2+\alpha},\quad\mbox{ for some }\alpha\in[0,1).

(C.14)

One can replace
R
with
R_{k}
in (
C.8
). Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{n}}-\nabla p(y)\cdot V(y)dy=

\displaystyle\lim_{R_{k}\rightarrow\infty}\int_{D(0,R_{k})}-\nabla p(y)\cdot V(y)dy.

As
V=0
on
\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}
one can write

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}p(y)\,V(y)\cdot e\,dy\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})\char 92\relax\partial\mathbb{H}_{n}}p(y)V(y)\cdot e\,dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{|z|=R_{k}\}}\left|p(y)V(y)\right|dy.

Then taking
q,q^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
, with
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
, and applying the Hölder inequality one obtains

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}p(y)V(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\{|z|=R_{k}\}}|p(y)|^{q}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\{|z|=R_{k}\}}|V(y)|^{q^{\prime}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}.

Then, estimate (
C.10
) yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}p(y)V(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\{|y|=R_{k}\}}|p(y)|^{q}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\{|y|=R_{k}\}}\frac{(y\cdot n)^{q^{\prime}}}{(|y|^{d})^{q^{\prime}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

and using the fact
0\leq y\cdot n\leq|y|=R_{k}
and estimate (
C.14
) we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}p(y)V(y)\cdot e\,dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\{|y|=R_{k}\}}|p(y)|^{q}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\{|y|=R_{k}\}}\frac{R^{q^{\prime}}_{k}}{R_{k}^{dq^{\prime}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(R_{k}^{d-2+\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(R_{k}^{q^{\prime}-dq^{\prime}}R_{k}^{d-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq CR_{k}^{\frac{d}{q}+\frac{\alpha-1}{q}-\frac{1}{q}}R_{k}^{1-d}R_{k}^{\frac{d}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq CR_{k}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{q}},

where
\alpha<1
and
C
is independent of
R_{k}
.
Thus, it follows from the last inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial D(0,R_{k})}p(y)V(y)\cdot e\,dy\right|\xrightarrow{R_{k}\rightarrow\infty}0.

Appendix D
Quasiperiodicity of
\partial_{y_{\gamma}}u^{s}
and
p^{s}
on the hyperplane
\{y\cdot n-s=0\}

Let
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let the pair
(u^{\theta\cdot n},p^{\theta\cdot n})
satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\!y}\cdot A(y)\nabla_{\!y}u^{\theta\cdot n}+\nabla_{\!y}p^{\theta\cdot n}&=&0&\mbox{in }\{y\cdot n-\theta\cdot n>0\},\\
\nabla_{\!y}\cdot u^{\theta\cdot n}&=&0,&\\
u^{\theta\cdot n}(y)&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\{y\cdot n-\theta\cdot n=0\}.\end{array}\right.

For arbitrary
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we set

u^{h}:=u^{\theta\cdot n}(\cdot-h),\;p^{h}:=p^{\theta\cdot n}(\cdot-h)
. Then, by the periodicity of
A=A(y)
and
g=g(y)
, the pair
(u^{h},p^{h})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}-\nabla_{\!y}\cdot A(y)\nabla_{\!y}u^{h}+\nabla_{\!y}p^{h}&=&0&\mbox{in }\{y\cdot n-\theta\cdot n-h\cdot n>0\},\\
\nabla_{\!y}\cdot u^{h}&=&0,&\\
u^{h}(y)&=&g(y)&\mbox{on }\{y\cdot n-\theta\cdot n-h\cdot n=0\}.\end{array}\right.

Now one can observe that he pair
(u^{h},p^{h})
coincides with the solution
(u^{s},p^{s})
to problem (
1.1
) with
s=\theta\cdot n+h\cdot n
. Therefore, by the uniqueness of the solution we have

\displaystyle u^{(\theta+h)\cdot n}(y)

\displaystyle=u^{h}(y)

\displaystyle=u^{\theta\cdot n}(y-h),\qquad\mbox{ for all }\;y\cdot n-(\theta+h)\cdot n>0.

and

\displaystyle p^{(\theta+h)\cdot n}(y)

\displaystyle=p^{\theta\cdot n}(y-h),\qquad\mbox{ for all }\;y\cdot n-(\theta+h)\cdot n>0

In particular, for
y=\theta+h+tn,\;t>0
,
we get

\displaystyle u^{(\theta+h)\cdot n}(\theta+h+tn)

\displaystyle=u^{\theta\cdot n}(\theta+tn)

\displaystyle p^{(\theta+h)\cdot n}(\theta+h+tn)

\displaystyle=p^{\theta\cdot n}(\theta+tn).

Recalling from formula (
1.7
) the latter equalities are also written

\displaystyle V(\theta+h,t)

\displaystyle=V(\theta,t)

\displaystyle R(\theta+h,t)

\displaystyle=R(\theta,t)

where the pair
(V,R)
satisfies problem (
1.6
). Since this holds for arbitrary
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}

one concludes that
V(\theta,t)
and
R(\theta,t)
are periodic in
\theta
. Then the derivatives

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta_{\gamma}}V(\theta,t),\quad D_{\theta}V(\theta,t),\quad\partial_{t}V(\theta,t)

are also periodic in
\theta
. Now, one differentiates the identity

\displaystyle u^{s}(y)

\displaystyle=V(y-(y\cdot n-s)n,y\cdot n-s),

to get

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{\gamma}}u^{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\;\partial_{\theta_{\gamma}}V(y-(y\cdot n-s)n,y\cdot n-s)

\displaystyle-n_{\gamma}D_{\theta}V(y-(y\cdot n-s)n,y\cdot n-s)n

\displaystyle+n_{\gamma}\partial_{t}V(y-(y\cdot n-s)n,y\cdot n-s).

Now, it appears that when restricted to the hyperplane
\{y\cdot n-s=0\}

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{\gamma}}u^{s}(y)=

\displaystyle\;\partial_{\theta_{\gamma}}V(y,0)-n_{\gamma}D_{\theta}V(y,0)n+n_{\gamma}\partial_{t}V(y,0)

is quasiperiodic. Similarly, the identity

\displaystyle p^{s}(y)

\displaystyle=R(y-(y\cdot n-s)n,y\cdot n-s)

shows that
p^{s}(y)
is quasiperiodic on
\{y\cdot n-s=0\}
.
